# Live encoder notes

Captured from a real `tools/encode.py` run on `tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl`
(100 synthetic English docs, deliberately overlapping vocabulary across
three thematic clusters: cooking, weather, software releases).

This file is the contract retriever's task #20 benchmark harness consumes
when it picks shape parameters for MS MARCO / LoTTE. Update on every
encoder version bump.

## Source

- Fixture: `tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl`, generated by
  `tests/fixtures/live/build_docs.py` (seed 2026, 100 docs).
- Encoder: `colbert-ir/colbertv2.0` via pylate 1.4.0, torch 2.9.0,
  transformers 4.56.2 (pin set copied from sibling
  `services/warp-service/requirements.txt`).
- Reproduce:
  ```bash
  python tests/fixtures/live/build_docs.py
  python tools/encode.py --docs tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl \
      --out tests/fixtures/live/tokens.bin --device cpu --batch 16
  ```

## Encoder dtype + batch size (post-hackathon plan 08)

`tools/encode.py` exposes two flags whose auto-defaults follow `--device`:

| flag | cpu / cuda | mps |
|---|---|---|
| `--dtype` | fp32 | fp16 (`model.half()` after load) |
| `--encoder-batch-size` | 32 | 256 |

`--dtype auto` (default) picks fp16 only on `--device mps`; cpu/cuda stay
fp32. Pass `--dtype fp32` explicitly to opt out on MPS. tokens.bin is f32
regardless — the per-doc loop already does `.cpu().float()` before
normalisation, so the on-disk format is unchanged. We also run a 4-doc
warmup pass on MPS so the first real batch isn't measuring Metal kernel
compilation.

Pattern mirrors sibling `services/warp-service/server.py` lines 187–231,
which already runs `WARP_ENCODER_FP16=1` + `WARP_ENCODER_BATCH_SIZE=256`
on the same Apple Silicon hardware.

### Measured speedup (Apple Silicon, jina-colbert-v2-64)

End-to-end CLI wall-clock on `tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl` (100 docs,
1697 kept tokens), back-to-back so HF cache is warm:

| invocation                                 | wall   | speedup |
|--------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
| fp32, encoder-batch-size 32                | 12.4 s | 1.00×   |
| fp16, encoder-batch-size 256               |  9.6 s | 1.29×   |

Encode-loop only (model already loaded, includes warmup, excludes load):

| corpus                          | fp32 bs=32 | fp16 bs=256 | speedup |
|---------------------------------|-----------:|------------:|--------:|
| 100 short synthetic docs        |    1.42 s  |     0.65 s  |  2.17×  |
| 1000 real Jira docs (avg 2.7 kB)|  128.79 s  |    90.18 s  |  1.43×  |

The advertised 4-6× lever from plan 08's overview did not materialise on
this hardware / model: the live fixture is too small to saturate the GPU
(model load dominates wall-clock), and on the longer Jira docs the
encoder is bandwidth-bound rather than compute-bound. The speedup is
real but in the 1.3-2× band, not 4-6×. Worth re-measuring on the full
corpus + a larger ColBERT model where the matmuls dominate.

### FP32 vs FP16 numerical parity

10 docs encoded twice through the same path, cosine similarity computed
between corresponding L2-normalised token vectors:

| metric              | value    |
|---------------------|----------|
| n token pairs       | 173      |
| mean cosine         | 0.999998 |
| min cosine          | 0.999783 |
| worst per-doc min   | 0.999783 |

Well above the 0.998 floor — FP16 is safe to default-on for MPS.

## Output shape

| field            | value                                      |
|------------------|--------------------------------------------|
| format version   | 2                                          |
| dim              | 128                                        |
| n_docs           | 100                                        |
| n_tokens         | 1592                                       |
| avg tokens/doc   | 15.92                                      |
| file size        | 822 320 bytes (~803 KB)                    |
| dropped          | 0                                          |
| dtype            | f32                                        |
| encode wall-clock| ~20 s on Apple Silicon CPU (single batch)  |

## Vocabulary distribution

After applying ColBERT's keep-mask (skiplist drops punctuation; CLS, [D]
prefix, and SEP are kept) the corpus reduces to **91 distinct vocab IDs**
across 1592 token rows.

| n_j threshold | % of distinct vocab IDs |
|---------------|------------------------:|
| n_j ≥ 2       | 100.0 %                 |
| n_j ≥ 5       | 86.8 %                  |
| n_j ≥ 10      | 45.1 %                  |

Top 10 token IDs by frequency:

| token_id | n_j | surface form (BERT WordPiece) |
|---------:|----:|-------------------------------|
|     1996 | 166 | the                            |
|      101 | 100 | [CLS] (one per doc)            |
|    30523 | 100 | [D] (pylate doc-prefix)        |
|      102 | 100 | [SEP]                          |
|     1997 |  69 | of                             |
|     2003 |  69 | is                             |
|     1998 |  51 | and                            |
|     2007 |  51 | with                           |
|     2544 |  48 | morning                        |
|    17974 |  40 | recipe                         |

Implications retriever should know about for #20:

1. **CLS/[D]/SEP form a 3-way tie at n_j = n_docs.** TAC will allocate κ_j
   to these as if they were genuine "high-information" tokens. They are
   fine for the gather-phase ⟨q, c⟩ accumulator (paper §5.1) because every
   query also produces them, but be aware that their centroids will sit
   on the per-token cluster mean of an essentially uniform direction.
2. **No singletons in this fixture.** That's deliberate — it stresses the
   `n_j ≥ 2` aliasing path. On a real MS MARCO sample expect a long-tail
   of singletons that fall into TAC's micro tier (n_j < μ=128 → κ_j=1).
3. **Embedding dim is 128.** PQ M=32 subspaces gives 4 dims/subspace
   (paper §4 default) — confirmed compatible with the indexer's PQ trainer.
4. **ColBERTv2 is downloaded on first use.** First encode pays a
   ~one-time HuggingFace fetch into `~/.cache/huggingface`. Subsequent
   runs are warm.

## Provisioning a venv

The pinned set lives in `services/warp-service/requirements.txt` (sibling
project, already proven). For this repo the simplest path is to reuse
that venv — copying the requirements file to `tools/.venv` works too:

```bash
# Reuse:
ln -s ../../ir-expo/services/warp-service/.venv tools/.venv

# Or create fresh from the same pins:
uv venv --python 3.13 tools/.venv
uv pip install --python tools/.venv/bin/python \
    -r ../ir-expo/services/warp-service/requirements.txt
```

Either way, run encode through `tools/.venv/bin/python tools/encode.py …`.
The harness gate `-Dlive=true` reads `tests/fixtures/live/tokens.bin`
directly — it doesn't depend on the venv at Zig-build time.

## Live harness (Zig side)

- File: `tests/live/vocab_aliasing.zig`
- Build flag: `-Dlive=true` (wired in `build.zig`)
- Tests:
  1. `live: token dump parses with v2 magic + version` — sanity check.
  2. `live: ≥50% of distinct token_ids show n_j ≥ 2 (paper §3 vocab
     aliasing)` — observed **100.0%** (91/91) on this fixture.
  3. `live: tac.cluster runs end-to-end on the real corpus` — runs
     `tac.tac.cluster` with κ_total=92 (calibrated for the all-tail
     tier distribution of this small fixture, see budget calibration
     comment in the test). Observed WCSS_total ≈ 502.272.

Default `zig build test` (without `-Dlive`) stays Python-free and the
fixture binary is gitignored — contributors without torch/pylate
provisioned see no impact.

### Why κ_total=92 specifically

With n_distinct=91 vocab IDs and *all* of them in the µ=128 / τ=256
tail tier (90 micro at κ_j=1, 1 small at κ_j=2, 0 active), Phase 1's
tail-only κ-sum is 92. There is no active-tier slack to absorb extra
budget, so any κ_total > 92 hits `error.BudgetTooLarge` from Phase 4
(the cap n_j/κ_j ≥ θ=39 has no slack to grow into without active
tokens). When retriever's #20 picks budgets for MS MARCO they will
have plenty of active tokens (n_j ≥ τ) and can use κ_total in the
hundreds-of-thousands range.

## MLX path setup (post-hackathon plan-12)

`tools/encode_mlx.py` is an additive Apple-Silicon-native re-port of
encode.py via Apple's MLX framework. The 4-gate parity contract in
`tests/live/mlx_parity.py` is **green** at both fp32 and fp16 on the
100-doc fixture, with gates 1+2 also confirmed on 1000 real Jira docs.
Encode.py remains the cross-platform canonical path; encode_mlx.py is
the fast Apple-Silicon option.

### Dependencies

Two extra packages on top of the pylate set, gated to darwin/arm64 in
`tools/requirements.txt`:

```
mlx>=0.18; sys_platform == "darwin" and platform_machine == "arm64"
mlx-lm>=0.20; sys_platform == "darwin" and platform_machine == "arm64"
```

mlx-lm currently pulls a newer `transformers` and `huggingface-hub`. We
re-pin transformers to `<5.0` afterwards so pylate's loader path keeps
working — pylate 1.4.0 was validated against transformers 4.x. If a
future mlx-lm hard-requires transformers 5.x, the MLX path moves into a
separate venv rather than dragging pylate forward.

Verified pin set (May 2026):

| package | version |
|---|---|
| mlx | 0.31.2 |
| mlx-lm | 0.31.3 |
| transformers | 4.57.6 |
| pylate | 1.4.0 |
| torch | 2.9.0 |

### Provisioning

```bash
uv pip install --python tools/.venv/bin/python -r tools/requirements.txt
# mlx-lm may briefly upgrade transformers; the requirements.txt
# transformers<5.0 pin restores it on a re-run.
uv pip install --python tools/.venv/bin/python "transformers>=4.40,<5.0"
```

### Weight export

`tools/export_colbert_to_mlx.py` walks the pylate state_dict (BERT base +
final linear projection) and writes
`tools/.cache/<model>/mlx_weights.safetensors`. Run once per model, then
`encode_mlx.py` mmaps the safetensors directly.

### Running the parity harness

After provisioning + weight export:

```bash
zig build -Doptimize=ReleaseFast            # builds zig-out/bin/tac for gate 4
tools/.venv/bin/python tests/live/mlx_parity.py --mlx-parity --dtype fp16
```

The script encodes the 100-doc fixture through both paths, parses the
resulting `tokens.bin` v2 + the `.tac` index format directly, and exits
non-zero on any gate failure. Default `zig build test` does NOT run this;
parity testing is opt-in via the `--mlx-parity` flag.

# ir-multivector-retrieval

A from-paper reimplementation of **Tachiom** — *Efficient Multivector Retrieval with Token-Aware Clustering and Hierarchical Indexing* — in **Zig** for maximum single-core throughput.

- **Paper:** https://arxiv.org/html/2604.28142v1
- **Tweet that sparked this:** https://x.com/lateinteraction/status/2050055160634708328
- **Authors' Rust impl:** **not yet published** as of 2026-05-01. We work strictly from the paper.

## Targets to reproduce (Table 1, single-core retrieval)

| Dataset       | Quality target  | Tachiom reported | Our impl |
| ------------- | --------------- | ---------------: | -------- |
| MS MARCO-v1   | MRR@10 = 39.0   | 10 ms / query    | TBD      |
| MS MARCO-v1   | MRR@10 = 39.3   | 14 ms / query    | TBD      |
| LoTTE-pooled  | Success@5 = 67.5 | 11 ms / query   | TBD      |

Clustering target: 600M token vectors → 262K centroids in ~8 minutes on a 64-thread Xeon.

## Measured performance (Apple Silicon, 10 cores)

Two corpora on a real Czech Jira ticket archive, `jinaai/jina-colbert-v2-64`, dim=64.

### 2,000-doc chunk (117,645 token vectors, kappa=10,240, θ=10 paper-relax)

| Build phase       | 1 thread | 10 threads | Speedup |
|-------------------|---------:|-----------:|--------:|
| `tac.clusterFlat` |    218 ms |     321 ms | —    |
| residuals + norms |      9 ms |       4 ms | 2.4× |
| **`pq.train`**    |  42,575 ms |  **9,011 ms** | **4.7×** |
| `pq.encode`       |    820 ms |     296 ms | 2.8× |
| **`hnsw.build`**  |  3,838 ms |   **867 ms** | **4.4×** |
| serialise         |     16 ms |      24 ms | —    |
| **Total**         |  **47,477 ms** |  **10,527 ms** | **4.5×** |

### Full 26,678-doc corpus (1,521,797 token vectors, kappa=32,768, paper-strict μ/τ/ε/θ)

19,221 distinct token IDs < 39,020 paper-strict max → no relaxation needed.

| Phase | Time | Notes |
|---|---:|---|
| Metal encode (pylate, fp32, batch=64) | **48 min** | GPU-bound; FP16 + larger batch is 4-6× ahead |
| `tac.clusterFlat` (parallel) | 4.2 s | comptime-specialized distance kernels ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)) — 1.28× over the generic @Vector path |
| residuals + norms (parallel) | 24 ms | |
| **`pq.train` (parallel + work-stealing outer dispatch + comptime-specialized kernels)** | **74.9 s** | atomic-counter outer dispatch ([plan-10](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-10-pq-workstealing.plan.md)) plus comptime-specialized `vec.l2sq` for hot dims ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)) — every PQ encode/refit triple `(subspace × centroid × token)` hits `vec.l2sq` on a 2-element subspace; the inline dispatcher lets the call-site `a.len` propagate through the switch and DCE the rest. ~12 % off vs work-stealing-only baseline; byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10} preserved. |
| `pq.encode` (parallel) | 0.9 s | |
| **`hnsw.build` (parallel, 10 threads)** | **2.7 s** | chunked deferred-commit, paper-strict deterministic CSR ([plan-06](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-06-hnsw-parallel.plan.md)) |
| serialise | 0.15 s | |
| **Build total (10 threads)** | **1 min 23 s** | work-stealing pq.train ([plan-10](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-10-pq-workstealing.plan.md)) + comptime-specialized distance kernels ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)). 1.13× over the work-stealing-only baseline (1:34); index byte-equal vs the generic kernel. |
| **Search latency** | **~54 ms / query** | paper-strict single-core, `kappa_c=80, kappa_d=1000` |
| **Index size** | **75.8 MB** | for 26,678 Jira tickets |

Quality smoke on Czech queries:
- `"rozesílání hromadné pošty z eshop domény"` → top hit `jira:ABC-1` (DKIM/SPF newsletter ticket) — matches WARP / `setup-note.md` ground truth.
- 0 docs dropped during encode — pylate's ColBERTv2 path handles all 26k Jira tickets including Czech multilingual content cleanly.

### Optimization story

Build was 47.5 s single-thread on the 2 k chunk; we got it to 12.4 s by parallelising the four embarrassingly-parallel stages (TAC per-token, PQ train per-subspace, PQ encode per-token, residuals per-token). HNSW build was the next lever — at 26 k it was 17.0 s serial; we replaced it with a chunked deferred-commit parallel build (paper-strict deterministic) which lands at **2.8 s on 10 threads (≈6× speedup)**, saving 14 s on full-corpus build wall time. PQ training stayed dominant because M=32 subspaces / 10 threads with a static `chunk = ceil(32/10) = 4` left fast workers idle while slow ones ground out the last subspace in their chunk. **Plan-10** swapped the static chunker for a shared `std.atomic.Value(u32)` work-stealing counter — every worker pulls the next subspace via `fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index reaches `PQ_M`. Lands at **97.1 s on full-Jira (was 115.8 s, ≈16 % off)**; the gain is bounded because most subspaces converge in similar Lloyd-iter counts, so the slack the static chunker left on the table was real but smaller than the 30-40 % ceiling. **Plan-11** then comptime-specialized the distance kernels (`vec.dot`, `vec.l2sq`, `vec.normalizeInPlace`) for the four dims that actually appear in the codebase — 2/4 (PQ subspaces) and 64/128 (jina-colbert-v2-64, ColBERTv2.0). **Full-Jira build dropped from 1:34 → 1:23 (1.13×, ≈12 % off total wall time), with `pq.train` itself going 84.8 s → 74.9 s.** Index file is byte-equal vs the generic kernel (paper §9 single-thread retrieval semantics unchanged) and build is byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10}. Supporting microbench (median of 5, kmeans-assign k=256, the dominant `vec.l2sq` caller in pq.train): dim=2 1.14×, dim=64 1.53×, dim=128 1.42× — the per-call wins are larger than the full-build delta because the kernels also amortize fixed pq.train scaffolding (codebook init, partition counts, Lloyd convergence detection) that didn't get faster. The remaining levers, in order of payoff:

1. **Within-subspace k-means parallelism** — *infrastructure shipped ([plan-07](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-07-within-subspace-kmeans.plan.md))* but inactive at the typical 10-thread invocation: `kmeans.fit` takes `n_threads` and parallelises the per-vector argmin across static chunks while keeping the f32 reduction order serial-in-vector-order, so n_threads=1 ↔ n_threads=10 produce **byte-equal** centroids (paper-strict). At `--threads 10` on M=32 / 10 cores the subspace-level parallelism already saturates; passing more inner threads only oversubscribes. The within-subspace path gates on `n_threads > M`, so it activates when a user runs e.g. `--threads 64`.
2. **FP16 Metal inference + bigger batches in the encoder** — 4-6× on the 48-minute encode; the single biggest absolute win since encode is 95 % of total ingestion cost.

#### Why `pub inline fn` matters for the dispatcher

The plan-11 dispatcher in `src/util/vec.zig` looks innocuous:

```zig
pub inline fn l2sq(a: []const f32, b: []const f32) VecError!f32 {
    if (a.len != b.len) return error.LengthMismatch;
    return switch (a.len) {
        2 => /* hand-inlined scalar */,
        4 | 64 | 128 => l2sqComptime(a.len, a, b),
        else => l2sqGeneric(a, b),
    };
}
```

The `inline` is load-bearing. Without it, `switch (a.len)` is a runtime branch — and dim=2 (the PQ-subspace path that pq.train hits **billions of times**) regressed from **112 ns → 207 ns per kmeans-assign step (1.85× slower)** even with the dim=2 arm picking the comptime kernel. Adding `pub inline fn` lets the call-site's slice length (which pq.train's inner loop knows is constant 2) propagate through the switch; LLVM dead-code-eliminates every other arm and emits two scalar mul-adds. With `inline` the same workload is **98 ns (1.14× faster than the original generic kernel).** Same source, same kernels — only difference is whether the dispatcher can be folded into the caller. This is the kind of win that doesn't show up on a single-call microbench (where `a` and `b` are loop-invariant pointers and LLVM can hoist anything) but dominates real hot loops where the slice length is a compile-time constant at the call site but not at the function definition.

Same lesson applies to the `*Generic` fallbacks: marked `inline fn` so the unspecialized arm is also folded into the caller.

## Why Zig

- Single-core retrieval target → no GC pauses, predictable allocation, manual SIMD via `@Vector(N, T)`.
- `comptime` for codegen-style specialization (PQ subspace count, HNSW edge count, distance kernels per dim).
- Direct mmap of binary index files — zero-copy load.
- Reference impl (Rust + AVX2) is unpublished; we implement strictly from paper text.

## Strict-paper rule

When the paper specifies a parameter, **use that value exactly**. Defaults:

- TAC: `μ=128, τ=256, ε=4, θ=39`
- PQ: `M=32 subspaces, b=8 bits`
- HNSW: `M_hnsw=32, efc=1500, ef_s=1.5·κ_c`
- Retrieval grid: `κ_c ∈ {15,20,40,80,100,120}`, `κ_d ∈ {250,500,1000,2000,4000}`, `α ∈ {0.35,0.4,0.45,0.5}`

Where the paper is silent (e.g. tie-breaking in Phase 4 reconciliation, exact CP update rule), each implementation choice is documented in the source comment block beside it as a `# paper-gap:` note.

## Workflow

- **Push directly to `main`** once `zig build test` is green. No PR review for this repo.
- Commit progressively per component (k-means → TAC → PQ → HNSW → gather → refine → eval).
- Numerical correctness validated on synthetic datasets in unit tests before each commit.

## Encoder pipeline

ColBERTv2 inference stays in Python (pylate). It dumps a flat binary `tokens.bin + tokens.meta.json` that Zig mmaps. See `tools/encode.py` and `src/io/token_dump.zig`.

const std = @import("std");

pub fn build(b: *std.Build) void {
    const target = b.standardTargetOptions(.{});
    const optimize = b.standardOptimizeOption(.{});

    // Public library module — re-exports everything.
    const lib_mod = b.addModule("tac", .{
        .root_source_file = b.path("src/root.zig"),
        .target = target,
        .optimize = optimize,
    });

    // Executable module wires the CLI to the library.
    const exe_mod = b.createModule(.{
        .root_source_file = b.path("src/main.zig"),
        .target = target,
        .optimize = optimize,
    });
    exe_mod.addImport("tac", lib_mod);

    const exe = b.addExecutable(.{
        .name = "tac",
        .root_module = exe_mod,
    });
    b.installArtifact(exe);

    // `zig build run -- <args>`
    const run_cmd = b.addRunArtifact(exe);
    run_cmd.step.dependOn(b.getInstallStep());
    if (b.args) |args| run_cmd.addArgs(args);
    const run_step = b.step("run", "Run the tac binary");
    run_step.dependOn(&run_cmd.step);

    // `zig build test` — runs every test block reachable from root.zig + main.zig.
    const lib_tests = b.addTest(.{ .root_module = lib_mod });
    const run_lib_tests = b.addRunArtifact(lib_tests);

    const exe_tests = b.addTest(.{ .root_module = exe_mod });
    const run_exe_tests = b.addRunArtifact(exe_tests);

    const test_step = b.step("test", "Run unit tests");
    test_step.dependOn(&run_lib_tests.step);
    test_step.dependOn(&run_exe_tests.step);

    // Live tests gate Python/torch/pylate-dependent harnesses behind a build
    // option so the default `zig build test` stays Python-free for any
    // contributor who hasn't provisioned tools/.venv. Enable explicitly:
    //
    //   zig build test -Dlive=true
    //
    // Live test sources live under tests/live/ and import the public `tac`
    // module just like in-tree unit tests. Owner: primitives-engineer (task #24).
    const live = b.option(
        bool,
        "live",
        "Include live torch+pylate-dependent tests under tests/live/",
    ) orelse false;
    if (live) {
        const live_mod = b.createModule(.{
            .root_source_file = b.path("tests/live/vocab_aliasing.zig"),
            .target = target,
            .optimize = optimize,
        });
        live_mod.addImport("tac", lib_mod);
        const live_tests = b.addTest(.{ .root_module = live_mod });
        const run_live_tests = b.addRunArtifact(live_tests);
        test_step.dependOn(&run_live_tests.step);
    }

    // Per-dataset benchmark executables (paper §6 Table 1 reproduction harness).
    // Each consumes pre-encoded tokens.bin + qrels.tsv at runtime; not part of
    // `zig build test`. Invoke with e.g. `zig build run-bench_msmarco -- <args>`.
    // .zig sources are retriever-owned; build.zig wiring is lead-owned (#28).
    inline for (.{
        .{ "bench_msmarco", "benchmarks/msmarco_v1.zig" },
        .{ "bench_lotte", "benchmarks/lotte_pooled.zig" },
        .{ "microbench_vec", "benchmarks/microbench_vec.zig" },
    }) |entry| {
        const bench_mod = b.createModule(.{
            .root_source_file = b.path(entry[1]),
            .target = target,
            .optimize = optimize,
        });
        bench_mod.addImport("tac", lib_mod);
        const bench_exe = b.addExecutable(.{
            .name = entry[0],
            .root_module = bench_mod,
        });
        b.installArtifact(bench_exe);
        const run_bench = b.addRunArtifact(bench_exe);
        run_bench.step.dependOn(b.getInstallStep());
        if (b.args) |args| run_bench.addArgs(args);
        const bench_step = b.step("run-" ++ entry[0], "Run the " ++ entry[0] ++ " harness");
        bench_step.dependOn(&run_bench.step);
    }
}

//! Strict-paper hyperparameter constants.
//!
//! These mirror the defaults from arxiv 2604.28142v1 exactly. They are LEAD-OWNED
//! and must not be redefined elsewhere — every module imports from here.
//!
//! Configurable params (e.g. retrieval-time κ_c, κ_d, α) live in the eval/CLI
//! layer; only the strict paper defaults live in this file.

// TAC — paper §3
pub const TAC_MU: u32 = 128; // micro-token frequency threshold
pub const TAC_TAU: u32 = 256; // small-token frequency threshold
pub const TAC_EPSILON: u32 = 4; // floor on κ_j for active tokens
pub const TAC_THETA: u32 = 39; // min vectors per centroid (cap on κ_j)

// Product Quantization — paper §4
pub const PQ_M: u32 = 32; // PQ subspaces
pub const PQ_BITS: u32 = 8; // bits per code
pub const PQ_CENTROIDS: u32 = 1 << PQ_BITS; // 256

// HNSW over centroids — paper §4
pub const HNSW_M: u32 = 32; // edges per node
pub const HNSW_EFC: u32 = 1500; // construction-time efSearch

// Compute runtime ef from κ_c (paper §5.1: ef_s = 1.5 · κ_c)
pub fn hnswEfSearch(kappa_c: u32) u32 {
    return (kappa_c * 3) / 2;
}

// Storage / file format
pub const TOKEN_DUMP_MAGIC: [8]u8 = "TAC_TKN1".*;
pub const INDEX_MAGIC: [8]u8 = "TAC_IDX1".*;
pub const TOKEN_DUMP_VERSION: u32 = 2; // v1 → v2: real BERT vocab IDs (paper §3 fidelity, task #21)
pub const INDEX_VERSION: u32 = 2; // v1 → v2: doc_token_offsets section for O(1) refine norm lookup

test "ef_s = ceil(1.5 · κ_c) per paper §5.1" {
    const std = @import("std");
    try std.testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 22), hnswEfSearch(15));
    try std.testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 30), hnswEfSearch(20));
    try std.testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 60), hnswEfSearch(40));
    try std.testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 120), hnswEfSearch(80));
    try std.testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 150), hnswEfSearch(100));
    try std.testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 180), hnswEfSearch(120));
}

Cross-corpus spot-check on 1000 real Jira docs (gates 1 + 2 only):

|---|---|---|

Notable: **MLX is byte-deterministic across runs** at both fp32 and fp16
on Apple Silicon. encode.py under the current pin set (transformers 4.57)
is NOT — two back-to-back runs differ in ~99% of output floats with max
abs ~1.5e-6 (fp32 ε-class) because transformers' `mean_resizing` path
fires when pylate expands the BERT vocab to add ColBERT's `[D]` and
`[Q]` tokens, advancing the global RNG without seeding before any
forward pass. That drift is below the parity floor so it doesn't break
gate 2, but it does mean MLX is now the more reproducible backend.

### Measured speedup (post-plan-13)

End-to-end CLI wall-clock, back-to-back so HF cache + MLX kernels are warm.
Re-measured after plan-13 perf tuning (fused SDPA, dtype-specific weights,
larger batch defaults + opt-in --sort-by-length, vectorized keep gather +
bulk-write writer).

100-doc live fixture (1592 kept tokens, mostly short synthetic English):

| invocation                                                 | wall   | speedup |
|------------------------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
| encode.py fp32 cpu, batch=32                               |  4.10s | 1.00×   |
| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 (default, post-plan-13)      |  3.86s | 1.06×   |

1000-doc Jira subset (55202 kept tokens, avg ~2.7 kB per doc):

| invocation                                                 | wall   | speedup |
|------------------------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
| encode.py fp32 cpu, batch=32                               | 35.6s  | 1.00×   |
| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=32 (parity-harness config)       | 10.4s  | 3.4×    |
| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 (default, post-plan-13)      | 10.1s  | 3.5×    |
| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 + --sort-by-length           |  5.7s  | **6.2×** |

Apple Silicon thermals are noisy across back-to-back runs (a sustained
encode loop heats the SoC and back-to-back wall-clocks drift up by 30-50%
once the system throttles). The numbers above are the median of three
runs after a cooldown gap; numerically equivalent runs on a previously
hot SoC come out ~30-50% slower.

The 100-doc fixture is too small to amortize tokenizer + cold-start cost
(fixed overhead dominates wall — encode.py and encode_mlx.py converge
near 4s once the pylate model load + HF cache warm dominates). The
1000-doc Jira subset is closer to real workloads and shows MLX's actual
lever, especially with --sort-by-length where per-batch padding waste
collapses on the heterogeneous Jira length distribution.

#### Per-rec impact (plan-13)

Order applied: rec-03 (fused SDPA) → rec-02 (dtype-specific weights) →
rec-04 (batch + sort) → rec-05 (vectorized gather + bulk writer). Numbers
are on the 1000-doc Jira subset, fp16, batch=32 unless noted, measured
when the SoC was cool:

| rec | what changed                                | 1000-doc fp16 wall | delta vs prev |
|-----|---------------------------------------------|-------------------:|---------------|
| baseline (post-plan-12)                          | ~8.80s     | —             |
| rec-03 fused SDPA                                | ~7.84s     | ~11%          |
| rec-02 skip startup astype (dtype-specific weights) | ~8.4s   | noise band    |
| rec-04 batch=128 default                          | ~7.24s    | ~8%           |
| rec-04 + --sort-by-length                         | ~5.65s    | ~28% on top of rec-04 default |
| rec-05 vectorized gather + bulk writer            | within noise of rec-04 | <5% |

Honest takeaways:

* **Fused SDPA is the cleanest win** — single kernel call replaces a
  4-step manual attention, ~10% on the 1000-doc workload at zero risk.
* **Sort-by-length is the largest lever** when the corpus has length
  variance (Jira docs span 2-5× in token count). The benefit shrinks
  toward zero on uniform-length corpora.
* **Dtype-specific weights and bulk-write writer** are real but small on
  warm benches — they remove startup astype and per-float struct.pack
  hot paths that show up more on cold runs / large outputs respectively.
  Microbench: bulk writer is 30× faster on the write step alone (0.36s
  → 0.012s on 1000 × 55 × 128), but the write step is a small fraction
  of total wall.
* Compounded wins are NOT additive. Sort-by-length + fused SDPA share
  the same Amdahl envelope (both reduce attention compute). The
  realistic combined ceiling on this hardware is the ~6× we see at fp16
  with sort.

### Recommendation

- Use `tools/encode.py` on cpu / cuda when reproducibility across machine
  classes matters more than encode wall-clock; it is the canonical path
  and what every benchmark we publish was measured on.
- Use `tools/encode_mlx.py` on Apple Silicon when encode wall-clock
  matters: it is parity-tested, byte-deterministic across runs, and
  ~3–4× faster than encode.py on cpu fp32 / 1000-doc real corpora.
  Token IDs are byte-identical and per-token cosine ≥ 0.9998 vs the
  pylate path, so downstream TAC indexing is interchangeable in
  practice (gate 4 confirmed centroid cosine ≥ 0.99999 at the live
  fixture's kappa=92 budget).

## Failure modes worth flagging

- If a future pylate revision breaks the `model.tokenize` →
  `model.forward` → `model.skiplist_mask` path inside
  `tools/encode.py::encode_docs`, you'll see one of:
  - `error: TestExpectedApproxEqAbs` in the live harness (vocab id ↔
    embedding row drift).
  - `n_j ≥ 2` ratio drops below 50 % (skiplist regression — punctuation
    leaking through with positional or duplicated ids).
- If the encoder dim drifts away from 128, `tac.cluster` still runs but
  PQ trainer (paper §4 with M=32) needs to be re-checked: dim must be
  divisible by M.

     2	
     3	A from-paper reimplementation of **Tachiom** — *Efficient Multivector Retrieval with Token-Aware Clustering and Hierarchical Indexing* — in **Zig** for maximum single-core throughput.
     4

    14	| MS MARCO-v1   | MRR@10 = 39.3   | 14 ms / query    | TBD      |
    15	| LoTTE-pooled  | Success@5 = 67.5 | 11 ms / query   | TBD      |

    19	## Measured performance (Apple Silicon, 10 cores)
    20	
    21	Two corpora on a real Czech Jira ticket archive, `jinaai/jina-colbert-v2-64`, dim=64.
    22	
    23	### 2,000-doc chunk (117,645 token vectors, kappa=10,240, θ=10 paper-relax)
    24	
    25	| Build phase       | 1 thread | 10 threads | Speedup |
    26	|-------------------|---------:|-----------:|--------:|
    27	| `tac.clusterFlat` |    218 ms |     321 ms | —    |
    28	| residuals + norms |      9 ms |       4 ms | 2.4× |
    29	| **`pq.train`**    |  42,575 ms |  **9,011 ms** | **4.7×** |
    30	| `pq.encode`       |    820 ms |     296 ms | 2.8× |
    31	| **`hnsw.build`**  |  3,838 ms |   **867 ms** | **4.4×** |
    32	| serialise         |     16 ms |      24 ms | —    |
    33	| **Total**         |  **47,477 ms** |  **10,527 ms** | **4.5×** |
    34	
    35	### Full 26,678-doc corpus (1,521,797 token vectors, kappa=32,768, paper-strict μ/τ/ε/θ)
    36	
    37	19,221 distinct token IDs < 39,020 paper-strict max → no relaxation needed.
    38	
    39	| Phase | Time | Notes |
    40	|---|---:|---|
    41	| Metal encode (pylate, fp32, batch=64) | **48 min** | GPU-bound; FP16 + larger batch is 4-6× ahead |
    42	| `tac.clusterFlat` (parallel) | 4.2 s | comptime-specialized distance kernels ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)) — 1.28× over the generic @Vector path |
    43	| residuals + norms (parallel) | 24 ms | |
    44	| **`pq.train` (parallel + work-stealing outer dispatch + comptime-specialized kernels)** | **74.9 s** | atomic-counter outer dispatch ([plan-10](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-10-pq-workstealing.plan.md)) plus comptime-specialized `vec.l2sq` for hot dims ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)) — every PQ encode/refit triple `(subspace × centroid × token)` hits `vec.l2sq` on a 2-element subspace; the inline dispatcher lets the call-site `a.len` propagate through the switch and DCE the rest. ~12 % off vs work-stealing-only baseline; byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10} preserved. |
    45	| `pq.encode` (parallel) | 0.9 s | |
    46	| **`hnsw.build` (parallel, 10 threads)** | **2.7 s** | chunked deferred-commit, paper-strict deterministic CSR ([plan-06](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-06-hnsw-parallel.plan.md)) |
    47	| serialise | 0.15 s | |
    48	| **Build total (10 threads)** | **1 min 23 s** | work-stealing pq.train ([plan-10](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-10-pq-workstealing.plan.md)) + comptime-specialized distance kernels ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)). 1.13× over the work-stealing-only baseline (1:34); index byte-equal vs the generic kernel. |
    49	| **Search latency** | **~54 ms / query** | paper-strict single-core, `kappa_c=80, kappa_d=1000` |
    50	| **Index size** | **75.8 MB** | for 26,678 Jira tickets |
    51	
    52	Quality smoke on Czech queries:
    53	- `"rozesílání hromadné pošty z eshop domény"` → top hit `jira:ABC-1` (DKIM/SPF newsletter ticket) — matches WARP / `setup-note.md` ground truth.
    54	- 0 docs dropped during encode — pylate's ColBERTv2 path handles all 26k Jira tickets including Czech multilingual content cleanly.
    55	
    56	### Optimization story
    57	
    58	Build was 47.5 s single-thread on the 2 k chunk; we got it to 12.4 s by parallelising the four embarrassingly-parallel stages (TAC per-token, PQ train per-subspace, PQ encode per-token, residuals per-token). HNSW build was the next lever — at 26 k it was 17.0 s serial; we replaced it with a chunked deferred-commit parallel build (paper-strict deterministic) which lands at **2.8 s on 10 threads (≈6× speedup)**, saving 14 s on full-corpus build wall time. PQ training stayed dominant because M=32 subspaces / 10 threads with a static `chunk = ceil(32/10) = 4` left fast workers idle while slow ones ground out the last subspace in their chunk. **Plan-10** swapped the static chunker for a shared `std.atomic.Value(u32)` work-stealing counter — every worker pulls the next subspace via `fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index reaches `PQ_M`. Lands at **97.1 s on full-Jira (was 115.8 s, ≈16 % off)**; the gain is bounded because most subspaces converge in similar Lloyd-iter counts, so the slack the static chunker left on the table was real but smaller than the 30-40 % ceiling. **Plan-11** then comptime-specialized the distance kernels (`vec.dot`, `vec.l2sq`, `vec.normalizeInPlace`) for the four dims that actually appear in the codebase — 2/4 (PQ subspaces) and 64/128 (jina-colbert-v2-64, ColBERTv2.0). **Full-Jira build dropped from 1:34 → 1:23 (1.13×, ≈12 % off total wall time), with `pq.train` itself going 84.8 s → 74.9 s.** Index file is byte-equal vs the generic kernel (paper §9 single-thread retrieval semantics unchanged) and build is byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10}. Supporting microbench (median of 5, kmeans-assign k=256, the dominant `vec.l2sq` caller in pq.train): dim=2 1.14×, dim=64 1.53×, dim=128 1.42× — the per-call wins are larger than the full-build delta because the kernels also amortize fixed pq.train scaffolding (codebook init, partition counts, Lloyd convergence detection) that didn't get faster. The remaining levers, in order of payoff:
    59	
    60	1. **Within-subspace k-means parallelism** — *infrastructure shipped ([plan-07](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-07-within-subspace-kmeans.plan.md))* but inactive at the typical 10-thread invocation: `kmeans.fit` takes `n_threads` and parallelises the per-vector argmin across static chunks while keeping the f32 reduction order serial-in-vector-order, so n_threads=1 ↔ n_threads=10 produce **byte-equal** centroids (paper-strict). At `--threads 10` on M=32 / 10 cores the subspace-level parallelism already saturates; passing more inner threads only oversubscribes. The within-subspace path gates on `n_threads > M`, so it activates when a user runs e.g. `--threads 64`.
    61	2. **FP16 Metal inference + bigger batches in the encoder** — 4-6× on the 48-minute encode; the single biggest absolute win since encode is 95 % of total ingestion cost.
    62	
    63	#### Why `pub inline fn` matters for the dispatcher
    64	
    65	The plan-11 dispatcher in `src/util/vec.zig` looks innocuous:
    66	
    67	```zig
    68	pub inline fn l2sq(a: []const f32, b: []const f32) VecError!f32 {
    69	    if (a.len != b.len) return error.LengthMismatch;
    70	    return switch (a.len) {
    71	        2 => /* hand-inlined scalar */,
    72	        4 | 64 | 128 => l2sqComptime(a.len, a, b),
    73	        else => l2sqGeneric(a, b),
    74	    };
    75	}
    76	```
    77	
    78	The `inline` is load-bearing. Without it, `switch (a.len)` is a runtime branch — and dim=2 (the PQ-subspace path that pq.train hits **billions of times**) regressed from **112 ns → 207 ns per kmeans-assign step (1.85× slower)** even with the dim=2 arm picking the comptime kernel. Adding `pub inline fn` lets the call-site's slice length (which pq.train's inner loop knows is constant 2) propagate through the switch; LLVM dead-code-eliminates every other arm and emits two scalar mul-adds. With `inline` the same workload is **98 ns (1.14× faster than the original generic kernel).** Same source, same kernels — only difference is whether the dispatcher can be folded into the caller. This is the kind of win that doesn't show up on a single-call microbench (where `a` and `b` are loop-invariant pointers and LLVM can hoist anything) but dominates real hot loops where the slice length is a compile-time constant at the call site but not at the function definition.
    79	
    80	Same lesson applies to the `*Generic` fallbacks: marked `inline fn` so the unspecialized arm is also folded into the caller.
    81	
    82	## Why Zig
    83	
    84	- Single-core retrieval target → no GC pauses, predictable allocation, manual SIMD via `@Vector(N, T)`.
    85	- `comptime` for codegen-style specialization (PQ subspace count, HNSW edge count, distance kernels per dim).
    86	- Direct mmap of binary index files — zero-copy load.
    87	- Reference impl (Rust + AVX2) is unpublished; we implement strictly from paper text.
    88	
    89	## Strict-paper rule
    90	
    91	When the paper specifies a parameter, **use that value exactly**. Defaults:
    92	
    93	- TAC: `μ=128, τ=256, ε=4, θ=39`
    94	- PQ: `M=32 subspaces, b=8 bits`
    95	- HNSW: `M_hnsw=32, efc=1500, ef_s=1.5·κ_c`
    96	- Retrieval grid: `κ_c ∈ {15,20,40,80,100,120}`, `κ_d ∈ {250,500,1000,2000,4000}`, `α ∈ {0.35,0.4,0.45,0.5}`
    97	
    98	Where the paper is silent (e.g. tie-breaking in Phase 4 reconciliation, exact CP update rule), each implementation choice is documented in the source comment block beside it as a `# paper-gap:` note.
    99	
   100	## Workflow
   101	
   102	- **Push directly to `main`** once `zig build test` is green. No PR review for this repo.
   103	- Commit progressively per component (k-means → TAC → PQ → HNSW → gather → refine → eval).
   104	- Numerical correctness validated on synthetic datasets in unit tests before each commit.
   105

     1	//! Strict-paper hyperparameter constants.
     2	//!
     3	//! These mirror the defaults from arxiv 2604.28142v1 exactly. They are LEAD-OWNED
     4	//! and must not be redefined elsewhere — every module imports from here.
     5	//!
     6	//! Configurable params (e.g. retrieval-time κ_c, κ_d, α) live in the eval/CLI
     7	//! layer; only the strict paper defaults live in this file.
     8	
     9	// TAC — paper §3
    10	pub const TAC_MU: u32 = 128; // micro-token frequency threshold
    11	pub const TAC_TAU: u32 = 256; // small-token frequency threshold
    12	pub const TAC_EPSILON: u32 = 4; // floor on κ_j for active tokens
    13	pub const TAC_THETA: u32 = 39; // min vectors per centroid (cap on κ_j)
    14	
    15	// Product Quantization — paper §4
    16	pub const PQ_M: u32 = 32; // PQ subspaces
    17	pub const PQ_BITS: u32 = 8; // bits per code
    18	pub const PQ_CENTROIDS: u32 = 1 << PQ_BITS; // 256
    19	
    20	// HNSW over centroids — paper §4
    21	pub const HNSW_M: u32 = 32; // edges per node
    22	pub const HNSW_EFC: u32 = 1500; // construction-time efSearch
    23	
    24	// Compute runtime ef from κ_c (paper §5.1: ef_s = 1.5 · κ_c)
    25	pub fn hnswEfSearch(kappa_c: u32) u32 {
    26	    return (kappa_c * 3) / 2;
    27	}
    28	
    29	// Storage / file format
    30	pub const TOKEN_DUMP_MAGIC: [8]u8 = "TAC_TKN1".*;
    31	pub const INDEX_MAGIC: [8]u8 = "TAC_IDX1".*;
    32	pub const TOKEN_DUMP_VERSION: u32 = 2; // v1 → v2: real BERT vocab IDs (paper §3 fidelity, task #21)
    33	pub const INDEX_VERSION: u32 = 2; // v1 → v2: doc_token_offsets section for O(1) refine norm lookup

    11	## Source
    12	
    13	- Fixture: `tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl`, generated by
    14	  `tests/fixtures/live/build_docs.py` (seed 2026, 100 docs).
    15	- Encoder: `colbert-ir/colbertv2.0` via pylate 1.4.0, torch 2.9.0,

    17	  `services/warp-service/requirements.txt`).

    25	## Encoder dtype + batch size (post-hackathon plan 08)
    26	
    27	`tools/encode.py` exposes two flags whose auto-defaults follow `--device`:
    28	
    29	| flag | cpu / cuda | mps |
    30	|---|---|---|
    31	| `--dtype` | fp32 | fp16 (`model.half()` after load) |
    32	| `--encoder-batch-size` | 32 | 256 |
    33	
    34	`--dtype auto` (default) picks fp16 only on `--device mps`; cpu/cuda stay
    35	fp32. Pass `--dtype fp32` explicitly to opt out on MPS. tokens.bin is f32
    36	regardless — the per-doc loop already does `.cpu().float()` before
    37	normalisation, so the on-disk format is unchanged. We also run a 4-doc
    38	warmup pass on MPS so the first real batch isn't measuring Metal kernel
    39	compilation.
    40	
    41	Pattern mirrors sibling `services/warp-service/server.py` lines 187–231,
    42	which already runs `WARP_ENCODER_FP16=1` + `WARP_ENCODER_BATCH_SIZE=256`
    43	on the same Apple Silicon hardware.
    44	
    45	### Measured speedup (Apple Silicon, jina-colbert-v2-64)
    46	
    47	End-to-end CLI wall-clock on `tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl` (100 docs,
    48	1697 kept tokens), back-to-back so HF cache is warm:
    49	
    50	| invocation                                 | wall   | speedup |
    51	|--------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
    52	| fp32, encoder-batch-size 32                | 12.4 s | 1.00×   |
    53	| fp16, encoder-batch-size 256               |  9.6 s | 1.29×   |
    54	
    55	Encode-loop only (model already loaded, includes warmup, excludes load):
    56	
    57	| corpus                          | fp32 bs=32 | fp16 bs=256 | speedup |
    58	|---------------------------------|-----------:|------------:|--------:|
    59	| 100 short synthetic docs        |    1.42 s  |     0.65 s  |  2.17×  |
    60	| 1000 real Jira docs (avg 2.7 kB)|  128.79 s  |    90.18 s  |  1.43×  |
    61	
    62	The advertised 4-6× lever from plan 08's overview did not materialise on
    63	this hardware / model: the live fixture is too small to saturate the GPU
    64	(model load dominates wall-clock), and on the longer Jira docs the
    65	encoder is bandwidth-bound rather than compute-bound. The speedup is
    66	real but in the 1.3-2× band, not 4-6×. Worth re-measuring on the full
    67	corpus + a larger ColBERT model where the matmuls dominate.
    68	
    69	### FP32 vs FP16 numerical parity
    70	
    71	10 docs encoded twice through the same path, cosine similarity computed
    72	between corresponding L2-normalised token vectors:
    73	
    74	| metric              | value    |
    75	|---------------------|----------|
    76	| n token pairs       | 173      |
    77	| mean cosine         | 0.999998 |
    78	| min cosine          | 0.999783 |
    79	| worst per-doc min   | 0.999783 |
    80	
    81	Well above the 0.998 floor — FP16 is safe to default-on for MPS.

    98	
    99	After applying ColBERT's keep-mask (skiplist drops punctuation; CLS, [D]
   100	prefix, and SEP are kept) the corpus reduces to **91 distinct vocab IDs**
   101	across 1592 token rows.
   102	
   103	| n_j threshold | % of distinct vocab IDs |
   104	|---------------|------------------------:|
   105	| n_j ≥ 2       | 100.0 %                 |
   106	| n_j ≥ 5       | 86.8 %                  |
   107	| n_j ≥ 10      | 45.1 %                  |
   108	
   109	Top 10 token IDs by frequency:
   110	
   111	| token_id | n_j | surface form (BERT WordPiece) |
   112	|---------:|----:|-------------------------------|
   113	|     1996 | 166 | the                            |
   114	|      101 | 100 | [CLS] (one per doc)            |
   115	|    30523 | 100 | [D] (pylate doc-prefix)        |
   116	|      102 | 100 | [SEP]                          |
   117	|     1997 |  69 | of                             |
   118	|     2003 |  69 | is                             |
   119	|     1998 |  51 | and                            |
   120	|     2007 |  51 | with                           |
   121	|     2544 |  48 | morning                        |
   122	|    17974 |  40 | recipe                         |
   123	
   124	Implications retriever should know about for #20:
   125	
   126	1. **CLS/[D]/SEP form a 3-way tie at n_j = n_docs.** TAC will allocate κ_j
   127	   to these as if they were genuine "high-information" tokens. They are
   128	   fine for the gather-phase ⟨q, c⟩ accumulator (paper §5.1) because every
   129	   query also produces them, but be aware that their centroids will sit
   130	   on the per-token cluster mean of an essentially uniform direction.
   131	2. **No singletons in this fixture.** That's deliberate — it stresses the
   132	   `n_j ≥ 2` aliasing path. On a real MS MARCO sample expect a long-tail
   133	   of singletons that fall into TAC's micro tier (n_j < μ=128 → κ_j=1).
   134	3. **Embedding dim is 128.** PQ M=32 subspaces gives 4 dims/subspace
   135	   (paper §4 default) — confirmed compatible with the indexer's PQ trainer.
   136	4. **ColBERTv2 is downloaded on first use.** First encode pays a
   137	   ~one-time HuggingFace fetch into `~/.cache/huggingface`. Subsequent
   138	   runs are warm.
   139

   160	## Live harness (Zig side)
   161	
   162	- File: `tests/live/vocab_aliasing.zig`
   163	- Build flag: `-Dlive=true` (wired in `build.zig`)
   164	- Tests:
   165	  1. `live: token dump parses with v2 magic + version` — sanity check.
   166	  2. `live: ≥50% of distinct token_ids show n_j ≥ 2 (paper §3 vocab
   167	     aliasing)` — observed **100.0%** (91/91) on this fixture.
   168	  3. `live: tac.cluster runs end-to-end on the real corpus` — runs
   169	     `tac.tac.cluster` with κ_total=92 (calibrated for the all-tail
   170	     tier distribution of this small fixture, see budget calibration
   171	     comment in the test). Observed WCSS_total ≈ 502.272.
   172	
   173	Default `zig build test` (without `-Dlive`) stays Python-free and the
   174	fixture binary is gitignored — contributors without torch/pylate
   175	provisioned see no impact.
   176	
   177	### Why κ_total=92 specifically
   178	
   179	With n_distinct=91 vocab IDs and *all* of them in the µ=128 / τ=256
   180	tail tier (90 micro at κ_j=1, 1 small at κ_j=2, 0 active), Phase 1's
   181	tail-only κ-sum is 92. There is no active-tier slack to absorb extra
   182	budget, so any κ_total > 92 hits `error.BudgetTooLarge` from Phase 4
   183	(the cap n_j/κ_j ≥ θ=39 has no slack to grow into without active
   184	tokens). When retriever's #20 picks budgets for MS MARCO they will
   185	have plenty of active tokens (n_j ≥ τ) and can use κ_total in the
   186	hundreds-of-thousands range.
   187	
   188	## MLX path setup (post-hackathon plan-12)
   189	
   190	`tools/encode_mlx.py` is an additive Apple-Silicon-native re-port of
   191	encode.py via Apple's MLX framework. The 4-gate parity contract in
   192	`tests/live/mlx_parity.py` is **green** at both fp32 and fp16 on the
   193	100-doc fixture, with gates 1+2 also confirmed on 1000 real Jira docs.
   194	Encode.py remains the cross-platform canonical path; encode_mlx.py is
   195	the fast Apple-Silicon option.
   196	
   197	### Dependencies
   198	
   199	Two extra packages on top of the pylate set, gated to darwin/arm64 in
   200	`tools/requirements.txt`:
   201	
   202	```
   203	mlx>=0.18; sys_platform == "darwin" and platform_machine == "arm64"
   204	mlx-lm>=0.20; sys_platform == "darwin" and platform_machine == "arm64"
   205	```
   206	
   207	mlx-lm currently pulls a newer `transformers` and `huggingface-hub`. We
   208	re-pin transformers to `<5.0` afterwards so pylate's loader path keeps
   209	working — pylate 1.4.0 was validated against transformers 4.x. If a
   210	future mlx-lm hard-requires transformers 5.x, the MLX path moves into a
   211	separate venv rather than dragging pylate forward.

   217	| mlx | 0.31.2 |

   276	|---|---|---|
   277	| 1. token-id byte equality      | PASS (55202 ids) | PASS (55202 ids) |
   278	| 2. cosine                      | mean 0.999997, min 0.999803 | mean 0.999998, min 0.999970 |
   279	
   280	Notable: **MLX is byte-deterministic across runs** at both fp32 and fp16
   281	on Apple Silicon. encode.py under the current pin set (transformers 4.57)
   282	is NOT — two back-to-back runs differ in ~99% of output floats with max
   283	abs ~1.5e-6 (fp32 ε-class) because transformers' `mean_resizing` path
   284	fires when pylate expands the BERT vocab to add ColBERT's `[D]` and
   285	`[Q]` tokens, advancing the global RNG without seeding before any
   286	forward pass. That drift is below the parity floor so it doesn't break
   287	gate 2, but it does mean MLX is now the more reproducible backend.
   288	
   289	### Measured speedup (post-plan-13)
   290	
   291	End-to-end CLI wall-clock, back-to-back so HF cache + MLX kernels are warm.
   292	Re-measured after plan-13 perf tuning (fused SDPA, dtype-specific weights,
   293	larger batch defaults + opt-in --sort-by-length, vectorized keep gather +
   294	bulk-write writer).
   295	
   296	100-doc live fixture (1592 kept tokens, mostly short synthetic English):
   297	
   298	| invocation                                                 | wall   | speedup |
   299	|------------------------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
   300	| encode.py fp32 cpu, batch=32                               |  4.10s | 1.00×   |
   301	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 (default, post-plan-13)      |  3.86s | 1.06×   |
   302	
   303	1000-doc Jira subset (55202 kept tokens, avg ~2.7 kB per doc):
   304	
   305	| invocation                                                 | wall   | speedup |
   306	|------------------------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
   307	| encode.py fp32 cpu, batch=32                               | 35.6s  | 1.00×   |
   308	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=32 (parity-harness config)       | 10.4s  | 3.4×    |
   309	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 (default, post-plan-13)      | 10.1s  | 3.5×    |
   310	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 + --sort-by-length           |  5.7s  | **6.2×** |
   311	
   312	Apple Silicon thermals are noisy across back-to-back runs (a sustained
   313	encode loop heats the SoC and back-to-back wall-clocks drift up by 30-50%
   314	once the system throttles). The numbers above are the median of three
   315	runs after a cooldown gap; numerically equivalent runs on a previously
   316	hot SoC come out ~30-50% slower.
   317	
   318	The 100-doc fixture is too small to amortize tokenizer + cold-start cost
   319	(fixed overhead dominates wall — encode.py and encode_mlx.py converge
   320	near 4s once the pylate model load + HF cache warm dominates). The
   321	1000-doc Jira subset is closer to real workloads and shows MLX's actual
   322	lever, especially with --sort-by-length where per-batch padding waste
   323	collapses on the heterogeneous Jira length distribution.
   324	
   325	#### Per-rec impact (plan-13)
   326	
   327	Order applied: rec-03 (fused SDPA) → rec-02 (dtype-specific weights) →
   328	rec-04 (batch + sort) → rec-05 (vectorized gather + bulk writer). Numbers
   329	are on the 1000-doc Jira subset, fp16, batch=32 unless noted, measured
   330	when the SoC was cool:
   331	
   332	| rec | what changed                                | 1000-doc fp16 wall | delta vs prev |
   333	|-----|---------------------------------------------|-------------------:|---------------|
   334	| baseline (post-plan-12)                          | ~8.80s     | —             |
   335	| rec-03 fused SDPA                                | ~7.84s     | ~11%          |
   336	| rec-02 skip startup astype (dtype-specific weights) | ~8.4s   | noise band    |
   337	| rec-04 batch=128 default                          | ~7.24s    | ~8%           |
   338	| rec-04 + --sort-by-length                         | ~5.65s    | ~28% on top of rec-04 default |
   339	| rec-05 vectorized gather + bulk writer            | within noise of rec-04 | <5% |
   340

     1	//! src/index/pq.zig — Product Quantization (paper §4 + §5.3).
     2	//!
     3	//! Owner: indexer. Plan: 03-index-pq-hnsw-storage.plan.md.
     4	//!
     5	//! Paper-strict defaults (constants.zig): M=32 subspaces, b=8 bits → 32 bytes
     6	//! per encoded vector. With dim=128 (ColBERTv2), sub_dim = 4.
     7	//!
     8	//! "Homogeneous compression" (paper §4): residuals are L2-normalised before
     9	//! PQ training and encoding. Their original norms are persisted separately
    10	//! by the storage layer (see `storage.zig`'s `norms_off` section) and
    11	//! re-applied at decode time. This module operates only on already-
    12	//! normalised residuals — callers do the normalisation upstream.
    13	//!
    14	//! Layout (row-major):
    15	//!   codebook[m][c][k] = codebooks[((m * 256) + c) * sub_dim + k]
    16	//!
    17	//! Distance table layout (paper §5.3) — three-level for cache locality:
    18	//!   table[m][c][i] = ⟨ q_i_subspace_m , codebook[m][c] ⟩
    19	//!   linear        = ((m * 256) + c) * n_q + i
    20	//! Refine reads `table[base..][0..n_q]` contiguously per (m, c) hit.
    21	//!
    22	//! paper-gap §5.3: micro-block alignment for SIMD. We do NOT pad n_q here
    23	//! — alignment is a storage-layer concern. If a SIMD pass needs aligned
    24	//! micro-blocks later, allocate `ceil(n_q/lane)*lane` and zero the tail.
    25	
    26	const std = @import("std");
    27	const Allocator = std.mem.Allocator;
    28	const constants = @import("../constants.zig");
    29	const vec = @import("../util/vec.zig");
    30	const kmeans = @import("../tac/kmeans.zig");
    31	
    32	pub const PqError = error{
    33	    DimNotDivisibleByM,
    34	    DimMismatch,
    35	    EmptyCorpus,
    36	    OutBufferWrongSize,
    37	    NQMismatch,
    38	} || Allocator.Error || vec.VecError || kmeans.KMeansError || std.Thread.SpawnError;
    39	
    40	/// Trained PQ codebooks. Owner of `codebooks`; call `deinit`.
    41	pub const PQ = struct {
    42	    dim: u32,
    43	    sub_dim: u32,
    44	    /// Length = `constants.PQ_M * constants.PQ_CENTROIDS * sub_dim`.
    45	    codebooks: []f32,
    46	
    47	    pub fn deinit(self: *PQ, gpa: Allocator) void {
    48	        gpa.free(self.codebooks);
    49	        self.* = undefined;
    50	    }
    51	
    52	    pub inline fn codebookEntry(self: *const PQ, m: u32, c: u32) []const f32 {
    53	        const base = ((@as(usize, m) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS)) +
    54	            @as(usize, c)) * @as(usize, self.sub_dim);
    55	        return self.codebooks[base .. base + @as(usize, self.sub_dim)];
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    /// Encode a single residual into M = 32 codes (one byte per subspace).
    59	    pub fn encode(self: *const PQ, residual: []const f32, out: *[constants.PQ_M]u8) PqError!void {
    60	        if (residual.len != @as(usize, self.dim)) return error.DimMismatch;
    61	        var m: u32 = 0;
    62	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
    63	            const sub = residual[m * self.sub_dim ..][0..self.sub_dim];
    64	            var best_c: u32 = 0;
    65	            var best_d: f32 = std.math.inf(f32);
    66	            var c: u32 = 0;
    67	            while (c < constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) : (c += 1) {
    68	                const d = vec.l2sq(sub, self.codebookEntry(m, c)) catch unreachable;
    69	                if (d < best_d) {
    70	                    best_d = d;
    71	                    best_c = c;
    72	                }
    73	            }
    74	            out[m] = @intCast(best_c);
    75	        }
    76	    }
    77	
    78	    /// Decode M codes back into a `dim`-length residual approximation.
    79	    pub fn decode(self: *const PQ, codes: *const [constants.PQ_M]u8, out: []f32) PqError!void {
    80	        if (out.len != @as(usize, self.dim)) return error.DimMismatch;
    81	        var m: u32 = 0;
    82	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
    83	            const code: u8 = codes[m];
    84	            const src = self.codebookEntry(m, code);
    85	            const dst = out[m * self.sub_dim ..][0..self.sub_dim];
    86	            @memcpy(dst, src);
    87	        }
    88	    }
    89	
    90	    /// Build a per-query distance table. `out` length must be
    91	    /// `PQ_M * PQ_CENTROIDS * n_q` floats. Layout: [M][256][n_q].
    92	    pub fn buildDistanceTable(
    93	        self: *const PQ,
    94	        query_tokens: []const f32,
    95	        n_q: u32,
    96	        out: []f32,
    97	    ) PqError!void {
    98	        if (n_q == 0) return error.NQMismatch;
    99	        if (query_tokens.len != @as(usize, n_q) * @as(usize, self.dim))
   100	            return error.NQMismatch;
   101	        const expected_out: usize =
   102	            @as(usize, constants.PQ_M) *
   103	            @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) *
   104	            @as(usize, n_q);
   105	        if (out.len != expected_out) return error.OutBufferWrongSize;
   106	
   107	        var m: u32 = 0;
   108	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
   109	            var c: u32 = 0;
   110	            while (c < constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) : (c += 1) {
   111	                const code_vec = self.codebookEntry(m, c);
   112	                const base = ((@as(usize, m) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS)) +
   113	                    @as(usize, c)) * @as(usize, n_q);
   114	                var i: u32 = 0;
   115	                while (i < n_q) : (i += 1) {
   116	                    const q_sub = query_tokens[
   117	                        @as(usize, i) * @as(usize, self.dim) + m * self.sub_dim ..
   118	                    ][0..self.sub_dim];
   119	                    out[base + @as(usize, i)] = vec.dot(q_sub, code_vec) catch unreachable;
   120	                }
   121	            }
   122	        }
   123	    }
   124	};
   125	
   126	/// Linear index into a distance table laid out [M][256][n_q].
   127	pub inline fn tableIndex(n_q: u32, m: u32, code: u8, i: u32) usize {
   128	    return ((@as(usize, m) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS)) + @as(usize, code)) *
   129	        @as(usize, n_q) + @as(usize, i);
   130	}
   131	
   132	/// Read a table entry. Inlined for the hot refine loop.
   133	pub inline fn lookup(table: []const f32, n_q: u32, m: u32, code: u8, i: u32) f32 {
   134	    return table[tableIndex(n_q, m, code, i)];
   135	}
   136	
   137	/// Train M codebooks from a flat row-major matrix of normalised residuals.
   138	///
   139	/// Each subspace runs independent k-means with `k = 256`. Per-subspace seeds
   140	/// are derived from the master `seed` so a single change to `seed` shifts
   141	/// every subspace identically (deterministic across runs).
   142	///
   143	/// paper-gap: PQ training corpus sampling rule. We train on the full
   144	/// `residuals` slice the caller passes — the storage builder is responsible
   145	/// for sampling if the corpus is too large. Documented at the call site in
   146	/// `storage.zig`'s build pipeline.
   147	/// Train PQ codebooks with M=32 independent subspace k-means. Setting
   148	/// `n_threads > 1` parallelises the M subspaces across worker threads
   149	/// (paper §4 + §5.3 layout assumes per-subspace independence). Subspace
   150	/// k-means is the dominant cost on large corpora; this is the
   151	/// highest-leverage parallelism in the build pipeline.
   152	pub fn train(
   153	    residuals: []const f32,
   154	    dim: u32,
   155	    seed: u64,
   156	    n_threads: u32,
   157	    gpa: Allocator,
   158	) PqError!PQ {
   159	    if (dim == 0) return error.DimMismatch;
   160	    if (dim % constants.PQ_M != 0) return error.DimNotDivisibleByM;
   161	    if (residuals.len == 0) return error.EmptyCorpus;
   162	    if (residuals.len % @as(usize, dim) != 0) return error.DimMismatch;
   163	
   164	    const sub_dim: u32 = dim / constants.PQ_M;
   165	    const n_residuals: usize = residuals.len / @as(usize, dim);
   166	
   167	    const total_codebook_floats: usize =
   168	        @as(usize, constants.PQ_M) *
   169	        @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) *
   170	        @as(usize, sub_dim);
   171	    const codebooks = try gpa.alloc(f32, total_codebook_floats);
   172	    errdefer gpa.free(codebooks);
   173	
   174	    if (n_threads <= 1) {
   175	        // ---- Serial across subspaces; within-subspace parallelism
   176	        // (plan-07) — we pass `n_threads` straight through so a `--threads 1`
   177	        // build still benefits from kmeans-side parallelism if it is bumped
   178	        // upstream. Today this is the same 1; preserved for symmetry.
   179	        const sub_buf = try gpa.alloc(f32, n_residuals * @as(usize, sub_dim));
   180	        defer gpa.free(sub_buf);
   181	
   182	        var m: u32 = 0;
   183	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
   184	            try trainOneSubspace(residuals, dim, sub_dim, n_residuals, m, seed, n_threads, sub_buf, codebooks, gpa);
   185	        }
   186	    } else {
   187	        // ---- Parallel: work-stealing dispatch over M=32 subspaces. ----
   188	        // Plan-10: replaced the prior static `chunk = ceil(M/n_threads)` split
   189	        // with a shared atomic counter. The static chunker left fast workers
   190	        // idle while slow ones ground on harder subspaces — kmeans.fit per
   191	        // subspace converges in 5-25 Lloyd iterations and the variance
   192	        // dominated wall time at chunk=4. Each worker now pulls the next
   193	        // subspace via `next_subspace.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index
   194	        // reaches PQ_M. Disjoint codebook slices
   195	        // `codebooks[m*256*sub_dim..(m+1)*256*sub_dim]` mean monotonic
   196	        // ordering is sufficient — no false sharing, no read-after-write
   197	        // hazard between workers. Codebook layout is m-indexed (not
   198	        // completion-order-indexed) and per-subspace seeds are
   199	        // `base_seed +% m`, so output is byte-equal across n_threads.
   200	        const WorkerCtx = struct {
   201	            residuals: []const f32,
   202	            dim: u32,
   203	            sub_dim: u32,
   204	            n_residuals: usize,
   205	            seed: u64,
   206	            kmeans_n_threads: u32,
   207	            codebooks: []f32,
   208	            gpa: Allocator,
   209	            next_subspace: *std.atomic.Value(u32),
   210	            err_out: ?PqError,
   211	        };
   212	        const WorkerRunner = struct {
   213	            fn run(ctx: *WorkerCtx) void {
   214	                const sub_buf = ctx.gpa.alloc(f32, ctx.n_residuals * @as(usize, ctx.sub_dim)) catch |err| {
   215	                    ctx.err_out = err;
   216	                    return;
   217	                };
   218	                defer ctx.gpa.free(sub_buf);
   219	                while (true) {
   220	                    const m = ctx.next_subspace.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);

 succeeded in 0ms:
   220	                    const m = ctx.next_subspace.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);
   221	                    if (m >= constants.PQ_M) break;
   222	                    trainOneSubspace(ctx.residuals, ctx.dim, ctx.sub_dim, ctx.n_residuals, m, ctx.seed, ctx.kmeans_n_threads, sub_buf, ctx.codebooks, ctx.gpa) catch |err| {
   223	                        ctx.err_out = err;
   224	                        return;
   225	                    };
   226	                }
   227	            }
   228	        };
   229	
   230	        const real_threads = @min(n_threads, constants.PQ_M);
   231	        const ctxs = try gpa.alloc(WorkerCtx, real_threads);
   232	        defer gpa.free(ctxs);
   233	        const threads = try gpa.alloc(std.Thread, real_threads);
   234	        defer gpa.free(threads);
   235	
   236	        // Within-subspace parallelism (plan-07): when subspace-level
   237	        // parallelism already saturates the cores (n_threads <= real_threads
   238	        // = min(n_threads, PQ_M=32)) further inner workers only oversubscribe
   239	        // and lose to scheduling overhead — measured 113s → 121s at inner=10
   240	        // and 113s → 135s at inner=2 on M=32 / outer=10 / 10-core Apple Si.
   241	        // Reserve within-subspace parallelism for the case where the user's
   242	        // total thread budget exceeds M (n_threads > 32) — there's leftover
   243	        // budget to soak up inside each subspace's argmin loop.
   244	        const kmeans_inner: u32 = blk: {
   245	            if (n_threads <= real_threads) break :blk 1;
   246	            break :blk @max(1, n_threads / real_threads);
   247	        };
   248	
   249	        var next_subspace = std.atomic.Value(u32).init(0);
   250	        for (0..real_threads) |t| {
   251	            ctxs[t] = .{
   252	                .residuals = residuals,
   253	                .dim = dim,
   254	                .sub_dim = sub_dim,
   255	                .n_residuals = n_residuals,
   256	                .seed = seed,
   257	                .kmeans_n_threads = kmeans_inner,
   258	                .codebooks = codebooks,
   259	                .gpa = gpa,
   260	                .next_subspace = &next_subspace,
   261	                .err_out = null,
   262	            };
   263	            threads[t] = try std.Thread.spawn(.{}, WorkerRunner.run, .{&ctxs[t]});
   264	        }
   265	        for (threads) |th| th.join();
   266	        for (ctxs) |c| if (c.err_out) |err| return err;
   267	    }
   268	
   269	    return .{
   270	        .dim = dim,
   271	        .sub_dim = sub_dim,
   272	        .codebooks = codebooks,
   273	    };
   274	}
   275	
   276	/// Train one PQ subspace's codebook. Materialises the m-th sub_dim columns
   277	/// of every residual into `sub_buf`, runs k=256 k-means, copies centroids
   278	/// into `codebooks[m*256*sub_dim..]`. Pure for distinct `m`.
   279	///
   280	/// `kmeans_n_threads` controls within-subspace parallelism (plan-07). At 1
   281	/// the kmeans loop is byte-identical to the pre-parallel path; at >=2 the
   282	/// argmin-per-vector step is sliced across worker threads but produces
   283	/// byte-equal centroids across thread counts (paper-strict reproducibility).
   284	fn trainOneSubspace(
   285	    residuals: []const f32,
   286	    dim: u32,
   287	    sub_dim: u32,
   288	    n_residuals: usize,
   289	    m: u32,
   290	    seed: u64,
   291	    kmeans_n_threads: u32,
   292	    sub_buf: []f32,
   293	    codebooks: []f32,
   294	    gpa: Allocator,
   295	) PqError!void {
   296	    var i: usize = 0;
   297	    while (i < n_residuals) : (i += 1) {
   298	        const src = residuals[i * @as(usize, dim) + m * sub_dim ..][0..sub_dim];
   299	        const dst = sub_buf[i * @as(usize, sub_dim) ..][0..sub_dim];
   300	        @memcpy(dst, src);
   301	    }
   302	    var res = try kmeans.fit(sub_buf, sub_dim, .{
   303	        .k = constants.PQ_CENTROIDS,
   304	        .max_iters = 25,
   305	        .tol = 1e-4,
   306	        .seed = seed +% @as(u64, m),
   307	        .n_threads = kmeans_n_threads,
   308	    }, gpa);
   309	    defer res.deinit(gpa);
   310	
   311	    const codebook_base: usize =
   312	        @as(usize, m) *
   313	        @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) *
   314	        @as(usize, sub_dim);
   315	    @memcpy(
   316	        codebooks[codebook_base .. codebook_base + res.centroids.len],
   317	        res.centroids,
   318	    );
   319	}
   320	
   321	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   322	// TESTS
   323	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   324	
   325	const testing = std.testing;
   326	
   327	test "tableIndex matches the [M][256][n_q] layout" {
   328	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(usize, 0), tableIndex(8, 0, 0, 0));
   329	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(usize, 7), tableIndex(8, 0, 0, 7));
   330	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(usize, 8), tableIndex(8, 0, 1, 0));
   331	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(usize, 256 * 8), tableIndex(8, 1, 0, 0));
   332	}
   333	
   334	test "PQ defaults match paper §4" {
   335	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 32), constants.PQ_M);
   336	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 8), constants.PQ_BITS);
   337	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 256), constants.PQ_CENTROIDS);
   338	}

   345	    try testing.expectError(error.DimNotDivisibleByM, r);

   355	test "train + decode + encode round-trip on synthetic blobs" {
   356	    const a = std.testing.allocator;
   357	    // dim=32 → sub_dim=1 (the trivial case where each subspace has a
   358	    // single coordinate). 256 distinct values in each subspace exactly
   359	    // → reconstruction is *exact* (codebook contains every value).
   360	    const dim: u32 = 32;
   361	    const n: u32 = 300;
   362	    const buf = try a.alloc(f32, @as(usize, n) * @as(usize, dim));
   363	    defer a.free(buf);
   364	    var prng = std.Random.DefaultPrng.init(0x52017);
   365	    for (buf) |*x| x.* = prng.random().floatNorm(f32);
   366	    // Normalise to mimic post-residual unit-norm vectors.
   367	    try vec.normalizeRowsInPlace(buf, dim);
   368	
   369	    var pq = try train(buf, dim, 17, 1, a);
   370	    defer pq.deinit(a);
   371	
   372	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 32), pq.dim);
   373	    try testing.expectEqual(@as(u32, 1), pq.sub_dim);
   374	    try testing.expectEqual(
   375	        @as(usize, 32) * 256 * 1,
   376	        pq.codebooks.len,
   377	    );
   378	
   379	    // Round-trip: encode → decode is idempotent on the codeword. Re-encoding
   380	    // the decoded vector must produce the same code (decode lands you back
   381	    // on the exact codebook entry, so its closest centroid is itself).
   382	    const residual = try a.alloc(f32, dim);
   383	    defer a.free(residual);
   384	    @memcpy(residual, buf[0..dim]);
   385	
   386	    var codes_a: [constants.PQ_M]u8 = undefined;
   387	    try pq.encode(residual, &codes_a);
   388	
   389	    const decoded = try a.alloc(f32, dim);
   390	    defer a.free(decoded);
   391	    try pq.decode(&codes_a, decoded);
   392	
   393	    var codes_b: [constants.PQ_M]u8 = undefined;
   394	    try pq.encode(decoded, &codes_b);
   395	    try testing.expectEqualSlices(u8, &codes_a, &codes_b);
   396	}
   397	
   398	test "train: reconstruction MSE bounded on synthetic Gaussians" {
   399	    const a = std.testing.allocator;
   400	    const dim: u32 = 32;
   401	    const n: u32 = 1024;
   402	    const buf = try a.alloc(f32, @as(usize, n) * @as(usize, dim));
   403	    defer a.free(buf);
   404	    var prng = std.Random.DefaultPrng.init(0xC0DE);
   405	    for (buf) |*x| x.* = prng.random().floatNorm(f32) * 0.1;
   406	
   407	    var pq = try train(buf, dim, 7, 1, a);
   408	    defer pq.deinit(a);
   409	
   410	    var codes: [constants.PQ_M]u8 = undefined;
   411	    const decoded = try a.alloc(f32, dim);
   412	    defer a.free(decoded);
   413	
   414	    var total_mse: f64 = 0.0;
   415	    var i: u32 = 0;
   416	    while (i < n) : (i += 1) {
   417	        const v = buf[@as(usize, i) * dim .. (@as(usize, i) + 1) * dim];
   418	        try pq.encode(v, &codes);
   419	        try pq.decode(&codes, decoded);
   420	        const sq = try vec.l2sq(v, decoded);
   421	        total_mse += @as(f64, sq) / @as(f64, dim);
   422	    }
   423	    const avg_mse = total_mse / @as(f64, n);
   424	    // sub_dim=1, 256 codes covering N(0, 0.01) — quantisation error is
   425	    // tiny. Expect mean per-dim MSE well below 1e-3.
   426	    try testing.expect(avg_mse < 1e-3);
   427	}

The PQ outer work-stealing design is structurally deterministic because subspaces write by `m`, not by completion order. The risk I’m checking now is lower-level: allocator concurrency and the inner k-means path, because those are what the byte-equality claim depends on.
