     1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2	"""tests/live/mlx_parity.py — 4-gate parity contract for tools/encode_mlx.py.
     3	
     4	Run:
     5	    tools/.venv/bin/python tests/live/mlx_parity.py --mlx-parity
     6	
     7	Gates (each must pass; none skippable):
     8	  1. Token-id byte equality between encode.py and encode_mlx.py.
     9	  2. Per-token cosine ≥ 0.998 mean and ≥ 0.99 min on the live fixture.
    10	  3. Two-run MLX determinism — byte-equal, or ≤ small ULP drift per float.
    11	     (Note: the PyTorch encode.py baseline is itself non-deterministic
    12	     across runs by ~1.5e-6 abs, owing to transformers 4.57's
    13	     mean_resizing path firing during pylate's vocab expansion. This
    14	     gate measures MLX's own self-consistency, not parity vs that drift.)
    15	  4. Downstream TAC clustering: build a tac index from each token_dump
    16	     with identical params/seed; assert headers (kappa_total, dim, n_*),
    17	     and centroid arrays agree within 1e-3 cosine per centroid.
    18	
    19	The script does NOT auto-install MLX, the weight cache, or the venv —
    20	it asserts they exist and gives a clean error if not.
    21	
    22	Args:
    23	  --mlx-parity   required gate flag (mirrors -Dlive in zig)
    24	  --max-docs     subset for fast smoke runs (default: all 100 fixture docs)
    25	  --dtype        fp32 or fp16 (default fp16 — matches encode.py MPS default)
    26	"""
    27	from __future__ import annotations
    28	
    29	import argparse
    30	import json
    31	import math
    32	import os
    33	import struct
    34	import subprocess
    35	import sys
    36	import time
    37	from pathlib import Path
    38	
    39	REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
    40	TOOLS = REPO / "tools"
    41	VENV_PY = TOOLS / ".venv" / "bin" / "python"
    42	FIXTURE = REPO / "tests" / "fixtures" / "live" / "docs.jsonl"
    43	WEIGHTS_DIR = TOOLS / ".cache" / "colbert-ir__colbertv2.0"
    44	MLX_WEIGHTS = WEIGHTS_DIR / "mlx_weights.safetensors"
    45	
    46	
    47	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    48	# tokens.bin parser (mirrors src/io/token_dump.zig::parseBytes structurally;
    49	# kept dependency-free so this script doesn't pull torch / mlx in itself).
    50	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51	
    52	
    53	def parse_tokens_bin(path: Path) -> dict:
    54	    with path.open("rb") as f:
    55	        data = f.read()
    56	    if data[:8] != b"TAC_TKN1":
    57	        raise SystemExit(f"{path}: bad magic {data[:8]!r}")
    58	    version = struct.unpack("<I", data[8:12])[0]
    59	    if version != 2:
    60	        raise SystemExit(f"{path}: bad version {version}")
    61	    dim = struct.unpack("<I", data[12:16])[0]
    62	    n_docs = struct.unpack("<Q", data[16:24])[0]
    63	    n_tok = struct.unpack("<Q", data[24:32])[0]
    64	    if data[32] != 0:
    65	        raise SystemExit(f"{path}: dtype != f32")
    66	
    67	    pos = 40
    68	    offsets = list(struct.unpack(f"<{n_docs+1}Q", data[pos : pos + (n_docs + 1) * 8]))
    69	    pos += (n_docs + 1) * 8
    70	    ids = list(struct.unpack(f"<{n_tok}I", data[pos : pos + n_tok * 4]))
    71	    pos += n_tok * 4
    72	    vec_bytes = n_tok * dim * 4
    73	    vecs = struct.unpack(f"<{n_tok*dim}f", data[pos : pos + vec_bytes])
    74	    return {
    75	        "dim": dim,
    76	        "n_docs": n_docs,
    77	        "n_tok": n_tok,
    78	        "offsets": offsets,
    79	        "ids": ids,
    80	        "vecs": vecs,
    81	    }
    82	
    83	
    84	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    85	# tac index (.tac) header + centroids parser.
    86	#
    87	# Layout (src/index/storage.zig):
    88	#   header (112 bytes inside a 128-byte region)
    89	#     [8] magic "TAC_IDX1"
    90	#     u32 version
    91	#     u32 dim
    92	#     u32 kappa
    93	#     u32 pq_M
    94	#     u8  pq_bits
    95	#     u8[3] _pad
    96	#     u32 sub_dim
    97	#     u64 n_docs
    98	#     u64 n_tokens
    99	#     u64 centroids_off
   100	#     u64 hnsw_off, pq_off, ilist_off, doc_off, doc_tok_off, norms_off, footer_off
   101	#   <padding to 128>
   102	#   centroids: kappa * dim f32 row-major
   103	#   ...
   104	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   105	
   106	
   107	def parse_tac_header_centroids(path: Path) -> dict:
   108	    with path.open("rb") as f:
   109	        data = f.read()
   110	    if data[:8] != b"TAC_IDX1":
   111	        raise SystemExit(f"{path}: bad TAC index magic {data[:8]!r}")
   112	    pos = 8
   113	    version = struct.unpack("<I", data[pos : pos + 4])[0]; pos += 4
   114	    dim = struct.unpack("<I", data[pos : pos + 4])[0]; pos += 4
   115	    kappa = struct.unpack("<I", data[pos : pos + 4])[0]; pos += 4
   116	    pq_M = struct.unpack("<I", data[pos : pos + 4])[0]; pos += 4
   117	    pq_bits = data[pos]; pos += 1
   118	    pos += 3  # _pad
   119	    sub_dim = struct.unpack("<I", data[pos : pos + 4])[0]; pos += 4
   120	    n_docs = struct.unpack("<Q", data[pos : pos + 8])[0]; pos += 8
   121	    n_tokens = struct.unpack("<Q", data[pos : pos + 8])[0]; pos += 8
   122	    centroids_off = struct.unpack("<Q", data[pos : pos + 8])[0]; pos += 8
   123	
   124	    cent_bytes = kappa * dim * 4
   125	    centroids = struct.unpack(
   126	        f"<{kappa*dim}f", data[centroids_off : centroids_off + cent_bytes]
   127	    )
   128	    return {
   129	        "version": version,
   130	        "dim": dim,
   131	        "kappa": kappa,
   132	        "pq_M": pq_M,
   133	        "pq_bits": pq_bits,
   134	        "sub_dim": sub_dim,
   135	        "n_docs": n_docs,
   136	        "n_tokens": n_tokens,
   137	        "centroids": centroids,
   138	    }
   139	
   140	
   141	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   142	# Math helpers
   143	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   144	
   145	
   146	def cosine(a: tuple[float, ...], b: tuple[float, ...]) -> float:
   147	    if len(a) != len(b):
   148	        raise SystemExit(f"length mismatch {len(a)} vs {len(b)}")
   149	    na = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in a))
   150	    nb = math.sqrt(sum(x * x for x in b))
   151	    if na == 0.0 or nb == 0.0:
   152	        return 0.0
   153	    return sum(x * y for x, y in zip(a, b)) / (na * nb)
   154	
   155	
   156	def cosines_per_token(pt: dict, mlx: dict) -> list[float]:
   157	    if pt["n_tok"] != mlx["n_tok"] or pt["dim"] != mlx["dim"]:
   158	        raise SystemExit(
   159	            f"shape mismatch: pt n_tok={pt['n_tok']} dim={pt['dim']} vs "
   160	            f"mlx n_tok={mlx['n_tok']} dim={mlx['dim']}"
   161	        )
   162	    dim = pt["dim"]
   163	    out = []
   164	    for i in range(pt["n_tok"]):
   165	        a = pt["vecs"][i * dim : (i + 1) * dim]
   166	        b = mlx["vecs"][i * dim : (i + 1) * dim]
   167	        out.append(cosine(a, b))
   168	    return out
   169	
   170	
   171	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   172	# Subprocess helpers (encoders + tac index build)
   173	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   174	
   175	
   176	def run(cmd: list[str], *, cwd: Path | None = None) -> tuple[float, str]:
   177	    t0 = time.perf_counter()
   178	    res = subprocess.run(
   179	        cmd, cwd=str(cwd) if cwd else None, capture_output=True, text=True
   180	    )
   181	    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - t0
   182	    if res.returncode != 0:
   183	        raise SystemExit(
   184	            f"command failed (exit {res.returncode}):\n  {' '.join(cmd)}\n"
   185	            f"stderr:\n{res.stderr}\nstdout:\n{res.stdout}"
   186	        )
   187	    return elapsed, res.stderr
   188	
   189	
   190	def encode_pt(out: Path, max_docs: int | None, dtype: str) -> float:
   191	    args = [
   192	        str(VENV_PY),
   193	        str(TOOLS / "encode.py"),
   194	        "--docs", str(FIXTURE),
   195	        "--out", str(out),
   196	        "--device", "cpu",
   197	        "--dtype", dtype,
   198	        "--batch", "16",
   199	    ]
   200	    if max_docs is not None:
   201	        args += ["--max-docs", str(max_docs)]
   202	    elapsed, _ = run(args, cwd=REPO)
   203	    return elapsed
   204	
   205	
   206	def encode_mlx(out: Path, max_docs: int | None, dtype: str) -> float:
   207	    args = [
   208	        str(VENV_PY),
   209	        str(TOOLS / "encode_mlx.py"),
   210	        "--docs", str(FIXTURE),
   211	        "--out", str(out),
   212	        "--dtype", dtype,
   213	        "--batch", "32",
   214	        "--weights", str(MLX_WEIGHTS),
   215	    ]
   216	    if max_docs is not None:
   217	        args += ["--max-docs", str(max_docs)]
   218	    elapsed, _ = run(args, cwd=REPO)
   219	    return elapsed
   220	
   221	
   222	def tac_index(tokens_bin: Path, out_tac: Path, kappa: int, seed: int) -> None:
   223	    tac_bin = REPO / "zig-out" / "bin" / "tac"
   224	    if not tac_bin.exists():
   225	        # Build it once.
   226	        run(["zig", "build", "-Doptimize=ReleaseFast"], cwd=REPO)
   227	    if not tac_bin.exists():
   228	        raise SystemExit(f"tac binary not found at {tac_bin} after zig build")
   229	    run([
   230	        str(tac_bin), "index",
   231	        str(tokens_bin), str(out_tac),
   232	        "--kappa", str(kappa), "--seed", str(seed),
   233	    ], cwd=REPO)
   234	
   235	
   236	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   237	# Gates
   238	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   239	
   240	
   241	def gate1_token_ids(pt: dict, mlx: dict) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   242	    if pt["ids"] != mlx["ids"]:
   243	        diffs = sum(1 for a, b in zip(pt["ids"], mlx["ids"]) if a != b)
   244	        return False, f"token_ids differ: {diffs}/{len(pt['ids'])} positions"
   245	    if pt["offsets"] != mlx["offsets"]:
   246	        return False, "doc_offsets (CSR) differ"
   247	    return True, f"token_ids byte-equal ({len(pt['ids'])} ids; offsets equal)"
   248	
   249	
   250	def gate2_cosine(pt: dict, mlx: dict, mean_floor: float, min_floor: float) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   251	    cos = cosines_per_token(pt, mlx)
   252	    cos_sorted = sorted(cos)
   253	    n = len(cos)
   254	    mean = sum(cos) / n
   255	    cmin = cos_sorted[0]
   256	    p1 = cos_sorted[max(0, n // 100)]
   257	    msg = (
   258	        f"cosine mean={mean:.6f} min={cmin:.6f} p1={p1:.6f} "
   259	        f"max={cos_sorted[-1]:.6f} (n={n})"
   260	    )
   261	    if mean < mean_floor:
   262	        return False, msg + f" — mean below floor {mean_floor}"
   263	    if cmin < min_floor:
   264	        return False, msg + f" — min below floor {min_floor}"
   265	    return True, msg
   266	
   267	
   268	def gate3_determinism(out_a: Path, out_b: Path, max_docs: int | None, dtype: str) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   269	    encode_mlx(out_a, max_docs, dtype)
   270	    encode_mlx(out_b, max_docs, dtype)
   271	    a = parse_tokens_bin(out_a)
   272	    b = parse_tokens_bin(out_b)
   273	    if a["ids"] != b["ids"] or a["offsets"] != b["offsets"]:
   274	        return False, "two-run determinism: token_ids/offsets differ"
   275	
   276	    # Compare floats with strict byte-equality first; if that fails, fall
   277	    # back to ULP-class threshold (≤ 4 ULPs ≈ ~5e-7 abs for fp32 values
   278	    # near 0.1, OK for L2-normalised ColBERT vectors).
   279	    diffs = 0
   280	    max_abs = 0.0
   281	    for x, y in zip(a["vecs"], b["vecs"]):
   282	        if x != y:
   283	            diffs += 1
   284	            d = abs(x - y)
   285	            if d > max_abs:
   286	                max_abs = d
   287	    n = len(a["vecs"])
   288	    if diffs == 0:
   289	        return True, f"two-run byte-identical ({n} floats)"
   290	    # ULP-class threshold for fp32 vectors with |x| ≤ 1 (L2-normalised, dim=128):
   291	    # 1 ULP ≈ 2**-23 ≈ 1.19e-7. Allow ≤ 4 ULPs.
   292	    threshold = 5e-7
   293	    if max_abs <= threshold:
   294	        return True, (
   295	            f"two-run within ULP-class threshold "
   296	            f"({diffs}/{n} floats differ; max abs {max_abs:.2e} ≤ {threshold:.2e})"
   297	        )
   298	    return False, (
   299	        f"two-run determinism failed: {diffs}/{n} floats differ; "
   300	        f"max abs {max_abs:.2e} > {threshold:.2e}"
   301	    )
   302	
   303	
   304	def gate4_clustering(pt_bin: Path, mlx_bin: Path, kappa: int, seed: int) -> tuple[bool, str]:
   305	    pt_tac = pt_bin.with_suffix(".pt.tac")
   306	    mlx_tac = mlx_bin.with_suffix(".mlx.tac")
   307	    tac_index(pt_bin, pt_tac, kappa=kappa, seed=seed)
   308	    tac_index(mlx_bin, mlx_tac, kappa=kappa, seed=seed)
   309	
   310	    pt_idx = parse_tac_header_centroids(pt_tac)
   311	    mlx_idx = parse_tac_header_centroids(mlx_tac)
   312	
   313	    # Header agreement on kappa/dim/n_*.
   314	    for key in ("dim", "kappa", "n_docs", "n_tokens"):
   315	        if pt_idx[key] != mlx_idx[key]:
   316	            return False, f"tac header field {key!r} differs: pt={pt_idx[key]} mlx={mlx_idx[key]}"
   317	
   318	    # Per-centroid cosine similarity. Centroid c maps the same vocab id
   319	    # cluster on both sides (kappa_per_token is determined by token_id
   320	    # frequencies, which gate 1 already proved equal), so centroid i on
   321	    # both sides clusters the same token-id subset.
   322	    dim = pt_idx["dim"]
   323	    kappa_v = pt_idx["kappa"]
   324	    pt_c = pt_idx["centroids"]
   325	    mlx_c = mlx_idx["centroids"]
   326	    cos_per_centroid = []
   327	    for i in range(kappa_v):
   328	        a = pt_c[i * dim : (i + 1) * dim]
   329	        b = mlx_c[i * dim : (i + 1) * dim]
   330	        cos_per_centroid.append(cosine(a, b))
   331	
   332	    cmin = min(cos_per_centroid)
   333	    cmean = sum(cos_per_centroid) / len(cos_per_centroid)
   334	    msg = (
   335	        f"tac kappa={kappa_v} dim={dim} centroid cosine mean={cmean:.6f} min={cmin:.6f}"
   336	    )
   337	    # Plan threshold: centroid means agree within 1e-3 cosine (so ≥ 1 - 1e-3 = 0.999).
   338	    if cmin < 0.999:
   339	        return False, msg + f" — min < 0.999"
   340	    return True, msg
   341	
   342	
   343	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   344	# Main
   345	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   346	
   347	
   348	def parse_args() -> argparse.Namespace:
   349	    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="MLX ↔ PyTorch parity for ColBERT encoder")
   350	    p.add_argument(
   351	        "--mlx-parity",
   352	        action="store_true",
   353	        required=True,
   354	        help="opt-in flag — mirrors -Dlive=true (parity tests are off-by-default)",
   355	    )
   356	    p.add_argument("--max-docs", type=int, default=None, help="subset for fast runs")
   357	    p.add_argument(
   358	        "--dtype",
   359	        choices=("fp32", "fp16"),
   360	        default="fp16",
   361	        help="encoder dtype on both sides (default fp16, matches MPS default)",
   362	    )
   363	    p.add_argument(
   364	        "--kappa",
   365	        type=int,
   366	        default=92,
   367	        help="tac index kappa for gate 4 (default 92, matches live harness)",
   368	    )
   369	    p.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=2026, help="tac index seed")
   370	    p.add_argument(
   371	        "--out-dir",
   372	        type=Path,
   373	        default=Path("/tmp"),
   374	        help="where to write the parity binaries",
   375	    )
   376	    p.add_argument(
   377	        "--skip-gate4",
   378	        action="store_true",
   379	        help="skip downstream cluster-overlap gate (useful when zig build isn't ready)",
   380	    )
   381	    return p.parse_args()
   382	
   383	
   384	def main() -> None:
   385	    args = parse_args()
   386	
   387	    # Sanity: required files exist.
   388	    for p in (FIXTURE, MLX_WEIGHTS, VENV_PY):
   389	        if not p.exists():
   390	            raise SystemExit(f"required path missing: {p}")
   391	
   392	    print(f"[parity] fixture: {FIXTURE} (max_docs={args.max_docs}) dtype={args.dtype}", flush=True)
   393	
   394	    pt_bin = args.out_dir / "parity_pt.bin"
   395	    mlx_bin = args.out_dir / "parity_mlx.bin"
   396	    mlx_a = args.out_dir / "parity_mlx_a.bin"
   397	    mlx_b = args.out_dir / "parity_mlx_b.bin"
   398	
   399	    print("[parity] encoding via encode.py (pylate, cpu)…", flush=True)
   400	    t_pt = encode_pt(pt_bin, args.max_docs, args.dtype if args.dtype == "fp32" else "fp32")
   401	    # Note: encode.py on cpu doesn't accept fp16 the same way; we pass fp32 there.
   402	    # MLX side runs at the requested dtype.
   403	    print(f"[parity] encode.py: {t_pt:.2f}s", flush=True)
   404	
   405	    print(f"[parity] encoding via encode_mlx.py ({args.dtype})…", flush=True)
   406	    t_mlx = encode_mlx(mlx_bin, args.max_docs, args.dtype)
   407	    print(f"[parity] encode_mlx.py: {t_mlx:.2f}s (vs {t_pt:.2f}s pt → {t_pt/max(t_mlx, 1e-9):.2f}x)", flush=True)
   408	
   409	    pt = parse_tokens_bin(pt_bin)
   410	    mlx = parse_tokens_bin(mlx_bin)
   411	
   412	    results: dict[str, tuple[bool, str]] = {}
   413	
   414	    print("[parity] gate 1: token-id byte equality")
   415	    results["gate1_token_ids"] = gate1_token_ids(pt, mlx)
   416	    print(f"  {'PASS' if results['gate1_token_ids'][0] else 'FAIL'}: {results['gate1_token_ids'][1]}")
   417	
   418	    # Gate 2 thresholds: when we compare fp32 PT vs fp{16,32} MLX the floors are 0.998 mean / 0.99 min.
   419	    print("[parity] gate 2: per-token cosine")
   420	    results["gate2_cosine"] = gate2_cosine(pt, mlx, mean_floor=0.998, min_floor=0.99)
   421	    print(f"  {'PASS' if results['gate2_cosine'][0] else 'FAIL'}: {results['gate2_cosine'][1]}")
   422	
   423	    print(f"[parity] gate 3: two-run MLX determinism ({args.dtype})")
   424	    results["gate3_determinism"] = gate3_determinism(mlx_a, mlx_b, args.max_docs, args.dtype)
   425	    print(f"  {'PASS' if results['gate3_determinism'][0] else 'FAIL'}: {results['gate3_determinism'][1]}")
   426	
   427	    if args.skip_gate4:
   428	        print("[parity] gate 4: skipped (--skip-gate4)")
   429	        results["gate4_clustering"] = (True, "skipped via --skip-gate4")
   430	    else:

codex

   181

   183	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
   184	            try trainOneSubspace(residuals, dim, sub_dim, n_residuals, m, seed, n_threads, sub_buf, codebooks, gpa);
   185	        }
   186	    } else {
   187	        // ---- Parallel: work-stealing dispatch over M=32 subspaces. ----
   188	        // Plan-10: replaced the prior static `chunk = ceil(M/n_threads)` split
   189	        // with a shared atomic counter. The static chunker left fast workers
   190	        // idle while slow ones ground on harder subspaces — kmeans.fit per
   191	        // subspace converges in 5-25 Lloyd iterations and the variance
   192	        // dominated wall time at chunk=4. Each worker now pulls the next
   193	        // subspace via `next_subspace.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index
   194	        // reaches PQ_M. Disjoint codebook slices
   195	        // `codebooks[m*256*sub_dim..(m+1)*256*sub_dim]` mean monotonic
   196	        // ordering is sufficient — no false sharing, no read-after-write
   197	        // hazard between workers. Codebook layout is m-indexed (not
   198	        // completion-order-indexed) and per-subspace seeds are
   199	        // `base_seed +% m`, so output is byte-equal across n_threads.
   200	        const WorkerCtx = struct {
   201	            residuals: []const f32,
   202	            dim: u32,
   203	            sub_dim: u32,
   204	            n_residuals: usize,
   205	            seed: u64,
   206	            kmeans_n_threads: u32,
   207	            codebooks: []f32,
   208	            gpa: Allocator,
   209	            next_subspace: *std.atomic.Value(u32),
   210	            err_out: ?PqError,
   211	        };
   212	        const WorkerRunner = struct {
   213	            fn run(ctx: *WorkerCtx) void {
   214	                const sub_buf = ctx.gpa.alloc(f32, ctx.n_residuals * @as(usize, ctx.sub_dim)) catch |err| {
   215	                    ctx.err_out = err;
   216	                    return;
   217	                };
   218	                defer ctx.gpa.free(sub_buf);
   219	                while (true) {
   220	                    const m = ctx.next_subspace.fetchAdd(1, .monotonic);
   221	                    if (m >= constants.PQ_M) break;
   222	                    trainOneSubspace(ctx.residuals, ctx.dim, ctx.sub_dim, ctx.n_residuals, m, ctx.seed, ctx.kmeans_n_threads, sub_buf, ctx.codebooks, ctx.gpa) catch |err| {
   223	                        ctx.err_out = err;
   224	                        return;
   225	                    };
   226	                }
   227	            }
   228	        };
   229	
   230	        const real_threads = @min(n_threads, constants.PQ_M);
   231	        const ctxs = try gpa.alloc(WorkerCtx, real_threads);
   232	        defer gpa.free(ctxs);
   233	        const threads = try gpa.alloc(std.Thread, real_threads);
   234	        defer gpa.free(threads);
   235	
   236	        // Within-subspace parallelism (plan-07): when subspace-level
   237	        // parallelism already saturates the cores (n_threads <= real_threads
   238	        // = min(n_threads, PQ_M=32)) further inner workers only oversubscribe
   239	        // and lose to scheduling overhead — measured 113s → 121s at inner=10
   240	        // and 113s → 135s at inner=2 on M=32 / outer=10 / 10-core Apple Si.
   241	        // Reserve within-subspace parallelism for the case where the user's
   242	        // total thread budget exceeds M (n_threads > 32) — there's leftover
   243	        // budget to soak up inside each subspace's argmin loop.
   244	        const kmeans_inner: u32 = blk: {
   245	            if (n_threads <= real_threads) break :blk 1;
   246	            break :blk @max(1, n_threads / real_threads);
   247	        };
   248	
   249	        var next_subspace = std.atomic.Value(u32).init(0);
   250	        for (0..real_threads) |t| {
   251	            ctxs[t] = .{
   252	                .residuals = residuals,
   253	                .dim = dim,
   254	                .sub_dim = sub_dim,
   255	                .n_residuals = n_residuals,
   256	                .seed = seed,
   257	                .kmeans_n_threads = kmeans_inner,
   258	                .codebooks = codebooks,
   259	                .gpa = gpa,
   260	                .next_subspace = &next_subspace,
   261	                .err_out = null,
   262	            };
   263	            threads[t] = try std.Thread.spawn(.{}, WorkerRunner.run, .{&ctxs[t]});
   264	        }
   265	        for (threads) |th| th.join();
   266	        for (ctxs) |c| if (c.err_out) |err| return err;
   267	    }
   268	
   269	    return .{
   270	        .dim = dim,
   271	        .sub_dim = sub_dim,
   272	        .codebooks = codebooks,
   273	    };
   274	}

   156	        wcss = 0.0;

   160	        // Phase 1: per-vector argmin. Optionally parallel across n_threads
   161	        // (plan-07). Pure per-vector so writes to `assignments` and
   162	        // `min_to_any` are byte-equal across thread counts.

   175	        // Phase 2: serial accumulation in vector order. Fixed reduction
   176	        // order keeps centroids byte-equal across n_threads (paper-strict).
   177	        for (0..n) |i| {
   178	            const a: usize = assignments[i];
   179	            wcss += min_to_any[i];
   180	            const v_i = vectors[i * d ..][0..d];
   181	            const sum_slot = cluster_sums[a * d ..][0..d];
   182	            for (0..d) |dd| sum_slot[dd] += v_i[dd];
   183	            cluster_counts[a] += 1;
   184	        }
   185	
   186	        iters_run = iter + 1;
   187	
   188	        // ---- Convergence check. ----
   189	        // Skip on first iter (prev_wcss == inf). Paper §3 does not pin a
   190	        // tolerance; relative delta is the standard choice.
   191	        if (iter > 0) {
   192	            const denom = if (prev_wcss > 0.0) prev_wcss else 1.0;
   193	            const rel_delta = (prev_wcss - wcss) / denom;
   194	            if (rel_delta < p.tol) break;
   195	        }
   196	
   197	        // ---- Update step. ----
   198	        for (0..k) |cc| {
   199	            if (cluster_counts[cc] > 0) {
   200	                const inv: f32 = 1.0 / @as(f32, @floatFromInt(cluster_counts[cc]));
   201	                const slot = centroids[cc * d ..][0..d];
   202	                const sum_slot = cluster_sums[cc * d ..][0..d];
   203	                for (0..d) |dd| slot[dd] = sum_slot[dd] * inv;
   204	            } else {
   205	                // Empty cluster — re-seed with farthest-from-any-centroid
   206	                // vector. paper-gap: paper does not specify; this is the
   207	                // canonical deterministic fix-up.
   208	                const far_idx = try vec.argmax(min_to_any);
   209	                @memcpy(

   211	                    vectors[far_idx * d ..][0..d],

   213	                // Mark this vector as "claimed" so a second empty cluster in
   214	                // the same iteration doesn't pick the same point.
   215	                min_to_any[far_idx] = -std.math.inf(f32);
   216	            }
   217	        }
   218	
   219	        prev_wcss = wcss;
   220	    }

   223	        .centroids = centroids,
   224	        .assignments = assignments,
   225	        .wcss = wcss,

    44	    /// Worker threads for the per-token Lloyd loop. `1` is serial
    45	    /// (preserves all existing test invariants and determinism). >1
    46	    /// dispatches via std.Thread.Pool — paper §3 says per-token

    53	pub const ClusteringError = error{
    54	    BudgetTooSmall,
   250	/// paper-gap: paper §3.3 says only "redistribute surplus/deficit". We implement
   251	/// the canonical Hamilton largest-remainder rule, deterministically tie-broken
   252	/// by token_id so two runs always land identically:
   253	///
   254	///   SURPLUS (delta > 0):
   255	///     Among active tokens with κ_j < cap_j (room to grow), give one centroid
   256	///     to whichever has the largest fractional remainder frac[j]. Ties broken
   257	///     by smallest token_id. Repeat until either delta == 0 or every active
   258	///     token is at its cap.
   259	///
   260	///   DEFICIT (delta < 0):
   261	///     Among active tokens with κ_j > epsilon (room to shrink), take one
   262	///     centroid from whichever has the smallest frac[j]. Ties broken by
   263	///     smallest token_id. Repeat until either delta == 0 or every active
   264	///     token is at its floor.
   265	///
   266	///     If we exhaust active room and still have deficit, we degrade tail tokens:
   267	///     small (κ_j=2) → 1, then micro (κ_j=1) → 0. paper-gap; never happens
   268	///     for paper-default parameters in production but kept for robustness.
   269	///
   270	/// Termination: each loop iteration changes exactly one κ_j by ±1 toward the
   271	/// goal. The total work is bounded by |delta| ≤ kappa_total, so worst case
   272	/// O(kappa_total · N_T) — fine since N_T is small (vocab ≤ 30k for ColBERT).
   273	///
   274	/// `error.BudgetTooLarge` if the total cap-room is insufficient for surplus.
   275	pub fn reconcilePhase4(
   276	    freqs: []const u32,
   277	    kappa: []u32,
   278	    frac: []const f32,
   279	    kappa_total: u32,
   280	    mu: u32,
   281	    tau: u32,
   282	    epsilon: u32,
   283	    theta: u32,
   284	) ClusteringError!void {
   285	    if (freqs.len != kappa.len or freqs.len != frac.len) return error.InvalidThresholds;
   286	
   287	    var current: u64 = 0;
   288	    for (kappa) |k| current += k;
   289	
   290	    if (current == kappa_total) return;
   291	
   292	    if (current < kappa_total) {
   293	        // SURPLUS — grow.
   294	        var remaining: u32 = @intCast(kappa_total - current);
   295	        while (remaining > 0) {
   296	            // Find active token with largest frac[j] subject to κ_j < cap_j.
   297	            var best: ?usize = null;
   298	            var best_frac: f32 = -std.math.inf(f32);
   299	            for (freqs, 0..) |n_j, j| {
   300	                if (classifyToken(n_j, mu, tau) != .active) continue;
   301	                const cap: u32 = n_j / theta;
   302	                if (kappa[j] >= cap) continue;
   303	                if (frac[j] > best_frac or (frac[j] == best_frac and best == null)) {
   304	                    best = j;
   305	                    best_frac = frac[j];
   306	                }
   307	            }
   308	            if (best) |idx| {
   309	                kappa[idx] += 1;
   310	                remaining -= 1;
   311	            } else {
   312	                return error.BudgetTooLarge;
   313	            }
   314	        }
   315	        return;
   706	    } else {
   707	        // ---- Parallel path (paper §3 "independent κ_j-means per token"). ----
   708	        // Static chunk distribution: each of n_threads threads owns a
   709	        // contiguous slice of token IDs [lo, hi) and processes them
   710	        // sequentially with one persistent gather buffer per thread. This
   711	        // avoids std.Thread.Pool (gone in Zig 0.16; std.Io.Group replaces it
   712	        // but threading an Io through TAC is invasive). Load balance is
   713	        // good for 8K+ tokens × 10 threads since per-token work varies but
   714	        // averages by law of large numbers; if we ever profile and find
   715	        // tail tokens dominating, switch to a work-stealing queue.
   716	        const n_threads = p.n_threads;

   720	            group_indices: []const u32,

   730	            err_out: ?ClusteringError,
   731	            lo: usize,
   732	            hi: usize,
   733	        };
   734

   736	            fn run(ctx: *ChunkCtx) void {
   737	                const gather = ctx.gpa.alloc(f32, @as(usize, ctx.max_n_j) * ctx.dim) catch |err| {
   738	                    ctx.err_out = err;
   739	                    return;
   740	                };
   741	                defer ctx.gpa.free(gather);
   742

   744	                var j = ctx.lo;
   745	                while (j < ctx.hi) : (j += 1) {
   746	                    const k_j = ctx.kappa_per_token[j];
   747	                    if (k_j == 0) continue;
   748	                    const n_j = ctx.group_offsets[j + 1] - ctx.group_offsets[j];

   751	                        j,
   752	                        k_j,
   753	                        n_j,
   754	                        ctx.group_offsets,
   755	                        ctx.group_indices,
   756	                        ctx.vectors,
   757	                        ctx.dim,
   758	                        ctx.params,
   759	                        ctx.global_offsets[j],
   760	                        ctx.centroids,
   761	                        ctx.assignments,
   762	                        gather,
   763	                        ctx.gpa,
   764	                    ) catch |err| {
   765	                        ctx.err_out = err;
   766	                        return;
   767	                    };
   768	                    wcss += w;
   769	                }
   770	                ctx.wcss_out = wcss;
   771	            }
   772	        };

   775	        defer gpa.free(ctxs);
   776	        const threads = try gpa.alloc(std.Thread, n_threads);
   777	        defer gpa.free(threads);
   778	
   779	        const chunk = (n_distinct + n_threads - 1) / n_threads;
   780	        for (0..n_threads) |t| {
   781	            const lo = t * chunk;
   782	            const hi = @min((t + 1) * chunk, n_distinct);
   783	            ctxs[t] = .{
   784	                .kappa_per_token = kappa_per_token,
   785	                .group_offsets = group_offsets,
   786	                .group_indices = group_indices,
   787	                .global_offsets = global_offsets,
   788	                .vectors = vectors,
   789	                .dim = dim,
   790	                .params = p,
   791	                .max_n_j = max_n_j,
   792	                .centroids = centroids,
   793	                .assignments = assignments,
   794	                .gpa = gpa,
   795	                .wcss_out = 0.0,
   796	                .err_out = null,
   797	                .lo = lo,
   798	                .hi = hi,
   799	            };
   800	            threads[t] = try std.Thread.spawn(.{}, ChunkRunner.run, .{&ctxs[t]});
   801	        }
   802	        for (threads) |th| th.join();
   803	
   804	        for (ctxs) |c| {
   805	            if (c.err_out) |err| return err;
   806	            wcss_total += c.wcss_out;
   807	        }
   808	    }
   809	
   810	    // Final invariant: every assignment is a valid centroid id.
   811	    for (assignments) |a| {
   812	        if (a >= total_centroids) return error.InvalidThresholds;
   813	    }

   207	    const ChunkErr = struct { e: ?@typeInfo(@typeInfo(@TypeOf(worker)).@"fn".return_type.?).error_union.error_set };
   208	    const Run = struct {
   209	        fn run(c: @TypeOf(ctx), lo: u64, hi: u64, out: *ChunkErr) void {
   210	            worker(c, lo, hi) catch |err| {
   211	                out.e = err;
   212	            };
   213	        }
   214	    };
   215	    const T = @min(n_threads, @as(u32, @intCast(n_tokens)));

 succeeded in 0ms:
    45	//!   (b) Layer-batched deferred-commit. Adopted. The algorithm:
    46	//!         1. Outer loop processes `q` in chunks of CHUNK_SIZE (=256) nodes.
    47	//!         2. Within a chunk, spawn `n_threads` workers; each worker handles a
    48	//!            slice of q values and runs greedy descent + searchLayer +
    49	//!            selectHeuristic against the **read-only** `adj` snapshot from
    50	//!            before the chunk started. Output per-q is a `TempInsertion`:
    51	//!              { q, l_new, top_layer, per-layer chosen[], cur_for_entry_update }.
    52	//!         3. After all workers join, a single-threaded commit pass walks the
    53	//!            TempInsertions in q-order and applies the mutations using the
    54	//!            existing append + shrinkNeighbours code. Because the commit pass
    55	//!            sees q-values in strictly ascending order, the resulting
    56	//!            adjacency lists are bit-identical to the serial path.
    57	//!
    58	//! Worker scratch:
    59	//!   * Each thread needs its own `visited_gen` + `generation` counter (the
    60	//!     serial path used a single shared buffer; per-worker copies are
    61	//!     allocated up-front and freed at chunk end).
    62	//!   * Each thread uses a thread-local arena for searchLayer scratch heaps so
    63	//!     we don't contend on the global allocator.
    64	//!
    65	//! Determinism guarantees:
    66	//!   * `pickLevel` is called once per q from the build's master Rng *before*
    67	//!     worker dispatch, so the level sequence is identical across thread counts.
    68	//!   * Each worker's selectHeuristic is deterministic (sort-by-sim with
    69	//!     stable ties; equal-sim entries kept in candidate-emission order which
    70	//!     itself is deterministic because searchLayer expands the frontier in a
    71	//!     fixed best-first order over a deterministic snapshot).
    72	//!   * The commit pass replays mutations strictly in q-order, so r_neighbours
    73	//!     receive incoming edges in the same order regardless of how many worker
    74	//!     threads computed the chosen[] sets.
    75	//!
    76	//! Read-only-snapshot caveat: a worker handling q' inside a chunk does NOT
    77	//! see edges committed by an earlier q < q' in the same chunk. The chunk
    78	//! size therefore trades parallelism for snapshot freshness — within a chunk
    79	//! every q reads the adj as it stood at chunk start. CHUNK_SIZE=256 keeps
    80	//! recall in line with the serial path (the recall@10 test on 1024 random
    81	//! vectors stays ≥ 0.9 for n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10}).
    82	//!
    83	//! Determinism scope:
    84	//!   * `n_threads=1` short-circuits the chunked path entirely and uses the
    85	//!     pre-parallel serial code. Bit-identical to today's CSR for all seeds.
    86	//!   * `n_threads>=2` uses the chunked path. The CSR is deterministic across
    87	//!     any thread count ≥ 2 (the determinism test pins n_threads=2 vs
    88	//!     n_threads=10 to byte-equal), but it differs from the serial path
    89	//!     because chunk-internal reads cannot see mid-chunk commits. This is
    90	//!     the standard parallel-HNSW determinism contract.
    91	
    92	const std = @import("std");
   294	/// Build an HNSW over `n = centroids.len / dim` L2-normalised vectors.
   295	///
   296	/// Determinism: for fixed `(centroids, dim, seed, params)` — and `n_threads`
   297	/// is part of `params` — the produced CSR is byte-identical across runs.
   298	/// `n_threads=1` matches a reference serial implementation byte-for-byte;
   299	/// `n_threads >= 2` uses chunked deferred-commit which produces a
   300	/// different-but-deterministic graph (chunks don't see same-chunk commits —
   301	/// standard parallel HNSW contract). Recall stays high; the bundled tests
   302	/// measure recall@10 ≈ 0.988 at n_threads=10 vs ≥ 0.9 floor.
   303	pub fn build(
   304	    centroids: []const f32,
   305	    dim: u32,
   650	    var entry_point = entry_point_ptr.*;
   651	    var max_level = max_level_ptr.*;
   652	
   653	    // Per-worker scratch (visited_gen + arena). Allocated once and reused
   654	    // across chunks; arena reset per-chunk to bound peak memory.
   655	    const n_threads: u32 = n_threads_param;
   656	    const scratches = try gpa.alloc(WorkerScratch, n_threads);
   657	    defer gpa.free(scratches);
   658	    var scratches_inited: u32 = 0;
   659	    errdefer {
   660	        var i: u32 = 0;
   661	        while (i < scratches_inited) : (i += 1) {
   662	            gpa.free(scratches[i].visited_gen);
   663	            scratches[i].arena.deinit();
   664	        }
   665	    }
   666	    {
   667	        var t: u32 = 0;
   668	        while (t < n_threads) : (t += 1) {
   669	            const vgen = try gpa.alloc(u32, n);
   670	            @memset(vgen, 0);
   671	            scratches[t] = .{
   672	                .visited_gen = vgen,
   673	                .generation = 0,
   674	                .arena = std.heap.ArenaAllocator.init(gpa),
   675	            };
   676	            scratches_inited = t + 1;
   677	        }
   678	    }
   679	    defer {
   680	        var i: u32 = 0;
   681	        while (i < n_threads) : (i += 1) {
   682	            gpa.free(scratches[i].visited_gen);
   683	            scratches[i].arena.deinit();
   684	        }
   685	    }
   686	
   687	    // Per-chunk plan storage. Each slot is one InsertPlan; chosen_per_layer
   688	    // pointers live in the worker arena (recycled per chunk).
   689	    const plans = try gpa.alloc(InsertPlan, CHUNK_SIZE);
   690	    defer gpa.free(plans);
   691	
   692	    const threads = try gpa.alloc(std.Thread, n_threads);
   693	    defer gpa.free(threads);
   694	
   695	    var q_base: u32 = 1;
   696	    while (q_base < n) {
   697	        // Adaptive chunk sizing: when the graph is small (q_base low) the
   698	        // snapshot captures most of the graph anyway, but workers handling
   699	        // late-chunk q's would see almost no committed edges. Growing the
   700	        // chunk geometrically — chunk = min(CHUNK_SIZE, q_base) — keeps
   840	
   841	        const slice_per_thread = (chunk_len + n_threads - 1) / n_threads;
   842	        var spawned: u32 = 0;
   843	        {
   844	            var t: u32 = 0;
   845	            while (t < n_threads) : (t += 1) {
   846	                const lo = t * slice_per_thread;
   847	                const hi = @min((t + 1) * slice_per_thread, chunk_len);
   848	                ctxs[t] = .{
   849	                    .centroids = centroids,
   850	                    .dim = dim,
   851	                    .adj = adj,
   852	                    .levels = levels,
   853	                    .ef_c = ef_c,
   854	                    .m = m,
   855	                    .scratch = &scratches[t],
   856	                    .plans = plans,
   857	                    .q_base = q_base,
   858	                    .lo = lo,
   859	                    .hi = hi,
   860	                    .snap_entry = snap_entry,
   861	                    .snap_max_level = snap_max_level,
   862	                    .err_out = null,
   863	                };
   864	                if (lo >= hi) continue; // empty slice, skip spawn
   865	                threads[spawned] = std.Thread.spawn(.{}, Runner.run, .{&ctxs[t]}) catch {
   866	                    // Join already-spawned and propagate.
   867	                    var j: u32 = 0;
   868	                    while (j < spawned) : (j += 1) threads[j].join();
   869	                    return error.ThreadSpawnFailed;
   870	                };
   871	                spawned += 1;
   872	            }
   873	        }
   874	        {
   875	            var j: u32 = 0;
   876	            while (j < spawned) : (j += 1) threads[j].join();
   877	        }
   878	        for (ctxs) |c| if (c.err_out) |err| return err;
   879	
   880	        // (4) Single-threaded commit pass in q-order.
   881	        const t_c: u64 = if (verbose) nowNs() else 0;
   882	        var ci: u32 = 0;
   883	        while (ci < chunk_len) : (ci += 1) {
   884	            const q_local: u32 = q_base + ci;
   885	            const plan = plans[ci];
   886	            var L_signed: i32 = plan.top_layer;
   887	            while (L_signed >= 0) : (L_signed -= 1) {
   888	                const L: u8 = @intCast(L_signed);
   889	                const chosen = plan.chosen_per_layer[L];
   890	                const cap_q: u32 = if (L == 0) m_max0 else m_max;
   891	
   892	                const q_neighbours = adj.neighbours(q_local, L);
   893	                try q_neighbours.ensureTotalCapacity(gpa, chosen.len);
   894	                for (chosen) |nbr_id| q_neighbours.appendAssumeCapacity(nbr_id);
   895	
   896	                for (chosen) |r_id| {
   897	                    const r_neighbours = adj.neighbours(r_id, L);
   898	                    try r_neighbours.append(gpa, q_local);
   899	                    if (r_neighbours.items.len > cap_q) {
   900	                        try shrinkNeighbours(
   901	                            centroids,
   902	                            dim,
   903	                            r_id,
   904	                            r_neighbours,
   905	                            cap_q,
   906	                            gpa,
   907	                        );
   908	                    }
   909	                }
   910	            }
   911	            if (plan.l_new > max_level) {
   912	                max_level = plan.l_new;
   913	                entry_point = q_local;
   914	            }
   915	        }
   916	        if (verbose) t_connect_ns.* += nowNs() - t_c;
   917	
   918	        // Phase A/B/heuristic timings are aggregated ad-hoc; for the parallel
   919	        // path we only report wall-clock (commit and the parallel section).
   920	        // The commit pass runs serially, so its time is counted accurately.
   921	        // The other accumulators stay at zero in parallel mode — verbose
   922	        // output prints n_threads so users know which mode produced the
   923	        // numbers.
   924	        _ = t_greedy_ns;
   925	        _ = t_search_ns;
   926	        _ = t_heuristic_ns;
   927	
   928	        q_base = q_end;
   929	    }

    65	    // Per-token max similarity (s̃_i(d)) and the running aggregate (S̃(q, d)).
    66	    // Dense f32 buffers are faster than a hash map for typical candidate
    67	    // counts (~10–50K on MS MARCO @ κ_c=80). Two parallel u8 "seen" bitmaps

    69	    // whether it ever made it into `touched_global`. We reset per_tok_seen
    70	    // entries from `touched_for_tok` so we never do an O(n_docs) clear.

   107	            for (index.ilists.list(j)) |d| {
   108	                if (!per_tok_seen[d]) {
   109	                    per_tok_seen[d] = true;
   110	                    per_token_max[d] = sim;
   111	                    try touched_for_tok.append(gpa, d);
   112	                } else if (sim > per_token_max[d]) {
   113	                    per_token_max[d] = sim;
   114	                }
   115	            }
   116	        }
   117	
   118	        for (touched_for_tok.items) |d| {
   119	            if (!global_seen[d]) {
   120	                global_seen[d] = true;
   121	                try touched_global.append(gpa, d);
   122	            }
   123	            accum[d] += per_token_max[d];
   124	        }

    75	///                      Pass-1 centroid term.
    76	///   per_token_max_buf - caller-provided scratch of length ≥ n_q. Reused
    77	///                      across all surviving candidates so refine itself
    78	///                      doesn't allocate.
    79	///
    80	/// The simpler fused-(k,i) loop is implemented first; the cache-optimised
    81	/// outer-m / inner-(k,i) restructuring (paper §5.3, "up to 3.8×") is a
    82	/// later optimisation gated on latency-harness evidence.
    83	pub fn refine(
    84	    index: *const storage.Index,
    85	    distance_table: []const f32,
    86	    query_tokens: []const f32,
    87	    n_q: u32,
    88	    candidate: gather.Candidate,
    89	    per_token_max_buf: []f32,
    90	) RefineError!f32 {
    91	    const dim: usize = @intCast(index.header.dim);
    92	    if (n_q == 0 or query_tokens.len != @as(usize, n_q) * dim) {
    93	        return error.QueryShapeMismatch;
    94	    }
    95	    const expected_table: usize =
    96	        @as(usize, constants.PQ_M) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) * @as(usize, n_q);
    97	    if (distance_table.len != expected_table) return error.TableSizeMismatch;
    98	    if (per_token_max_buf.len < n_q) return error.QueryShapeMismatch;
    99	    const n_docs: u32 = @intCast(index.header.n_docs);
   100	    if (candidate.doc_id >= n_docs) return error.DocOutOfRange;
   101	
   102	    const layout = try decodeDocLayout(index.docLayout(candidate.doc_id));
   103	    const n_d = layout.n_d;
   104	    const tok_range = index.docTokenRange(candidate.doc_id);
   105	    const norms_lo: usize = @intCast(tok_range[0]);
   106	    const norms_hi: usize = @intCast(tok_range[1]);
   107	    if (norms_hi - norms_lo != n_d) return error.DocLayoutCorrupt;
   108	    const norms = index.residual_norms[norms_lo..norms_hi];
   109	
   110	    // Initialise per-token max to -inf so the first valid score wins.
   111	    var i: u32 = 0;
   112	    while (i < n_q) : (i += 1) per_token_max_buf[i] = -std.math.inf(f32);
   113	
   114	    // Fused (k, i) loop — paper §5.3 Pass 1 + Pass 2 interleaved per token.
   115	    var k: u32 = 0;
   116	    while (k < n_d) : (k += 1) {
   117	        const c_id: u32 = layout.centroid_ids[k];
   118	        const c_vec = index.centroids[@as(usize, c_id) * dim ..][0..dim];
   119	        const norm_k = norms[k];
   120	        const code_base: usize = @as(usize, k) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_M);
   121	
   122	        i = 0;
   123	        while (i < n_q) : (i += 1) {
   124	            const q_i = query_tokens[@as(usize, i) * dim ..][0..dim];
   125	            const cent_term = try vec.dot(q_i, c_vec);
   126	
   127	            // Residual term: ⟨q_i, r_k⟩ via PQ-decoded codes.
   128	            var res_term: f32 = 0.0;
   129	            var m: u32 = 0;
   130	            while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
   131	                const code: u8 = layout.pq_codes[code_base + @as(usize, m)];
   132	                res_term += pq_mod.lookup(distance_table, n_q, m, code, i);
   133	            }
   134	
   135	            const score = cent_term + norm_k * res_term;
   136	            if (score > per_token_max_buf[i]) per_token_max_buf[i] = score;
   137	        }
   138	    }
   139	
   140	    // Aggregate. n_d == 0 → no per-token max was written; return 0 so the

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba src/retrieval/prune.zig | sed -n '15,75p'" in /Users/satan/side/experiments/ir-multivector-retrieval
 succeeded in 0ms:
    15	    /// `null` disables adaptive Candidates Pruning (top-κ_d only).
    16	    alpha: ?f32 = null,
    17	};
    18	
    19	fn cmpDesc(_: void, a: gather.Candidate, b: gather.Candidate) bool {
    20	    return a.score > b.score;
    21	}
    22	
    23	/// Mutate `candidates` in-place: sort descending by score and truncate to
    24	/// `≤ kappa_d` entries. If `alpha` is set, additionally drop the tail of
    25	/// candidates whose score falls below `alpha * candidates[0].score`.
    26	///
    27	/// Returns the surviving prefix slice (no allocations). Caller still owns
    28	/// `candidates`'s backing memory and should free the original allocation.
    29	///
    30	/// paper-gap §5.2: the paper says "using a fraction α of the running
    31	/// maximum" but doesn't formalise the running-max update rule. We use the
    32	/// simplest deterministic interpretation that matches the wording:
    33	///
    34	///   running_max := candidates[0].score   (the post-sort top)
    35	///   threshold   := alpha * running_max
    36	///   keep iff score >= threshold
    37	///
    38	/// Because Stage 1 leaves `candidates` sorted descending, Stage 2 collapses
    39	/// to a single-pass cutoff: walk until the first score < threshold, drop
    40	/// the rest. Deterministic at every (κ_d, α). Cross-check vs the reference
    41	/// Rust impl tracked in plan 06.
    42	pub fn prune(candidates: []gather.Candidate, params: PruneParams) []gather.Candidate {
    43	    if (candidates.len == 0 or params.kappa_d == 0) return candidates[0..0];
    44	
    45	    // Stage 1 — descending sort by score. pdq is O(n log n) with tiny
    46	    // constants and is deterministic across runs; quickselect to κ_d would
    47	    // shave a log factor but the gather output is bounded by candidate
    48	    // counts in the tens of thousands on MS MARCO @ κ_c=80, well within
    49	    // budget. Revisit if the latency harness shows prune dominating.
    50	    std.sort.pdq(gather.Candidate, candidates, {}, cmpDesc);
    51	
    52	    var keep: usize = @min(candidates.len, @as(usize, params.kappa_d));
    53	
    54	    // Stage 2 — adaptive Candidates Pruning.
    55	    if (params.alpha) |a| {
    56	        const top = candidates[0].score;
    57	        const threshold = a * top;
    58	        var cut: usize = keep;
    59	        var i: usize = 0;
    60	        while (i < keep) : (i += 1) {
    61	            if (candidates[i].score < threshold) {
    62	                cut = i;
    63	                break;
    64	            }
    65	        }
    66	        keep = cut;
    67	    }
    68	
    69	    return candidates[0..keep];
    70	}
    71

    73	// Tests
    74	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    75

    37	pub inline fn dot(a: []const f32, b: []const f32) VecError!f32 {
    38	    if (a.len != b.len) return error.LengthMismatch;
    39	    // dim=2 is hand-inlined here rather than going through `dotComptime(2,...)`:
    40	    // the indirection occasionally blocks LLVM from constant-folding the slice
    41	    // length all the way to two scalar mul-adds. Hand-inlining is unambiguous.
    42	    return switch (a.len) {
    43	        2 => a[0] * b[0] + a[1] * b[1],
    44	        4 => dotComptime(4, a, b),
    45	        64 => dotComptime(64, a, b),
    46	        128 => dotComptime(128, a, b),
    47	        else => dotGeneric(a, b),
    48	    };
    49	}
   109	pub inline fn l2sq(a: []const f32, b: []const f32) VecError!f32 {
   110	    if (a.len != b.len) return error.LengthMismatch;
   111	    return switch (a.len) {
   112	        // dim=2 directly inline — same plan-11 reasoning as `dot`. PQ encode
   113	        // hits this path on every (subspace × centroid × token) triple.
   114	        2 => blk: {
   115	            const d0 = a[0] - b[0];
   116	            const d1 = a[1] - b[1];
   117	            break :blk d0 * d0 + d1 * d1;
   118	        },
   119	        4 => l2sqComptime(4, a, b),
   120	        64 => l2sqComptime(64, a, b),
   121	        128 => l2sqComptime(128, a, b),
   122	        else => l2sqGeneric(a, b),
   123	    };
   183	pub inline fn normalizeInPlace(v: []f32) VecError!void {
   184	    if (v.len == 0) return error.EmptyInput;
   185	    switch (v.len) {
   186	        64 => normalizeComptime(64, v),
   187	        128 => normalizeComptime(128, v),
   188	        else => normalizeGeneric(v),
   189	    }
   190	}

     1	//! src/retrieval/search.zig — Retrieval.search driver (paper §5).
     2	//!
     3	//! Owner: retriever. Stitches gather → prune → refine into the public
     4	//! retrieval entry point.
     5	//!
     6	//! NOTE: this file is not yet wired into `src/root.zig`'s `retrieval` block.
     7	//! Adding the line is lead-owned (root.zig is a cross-cutting file). Until
     8	//! that lands, search() is callable from in-tree tests but doesn't appear
     9	//! on the public `tac.retrieval.*` namespace.
    10	
    11	const std = @import("std");
    12	const Allocator = std.mem.Allocator;
    13	
    14	const constants = @import("../constants.zig");
    15	const storage = @import("../index/storage.zig");
    16	const pq_mod = @import("../index/pq.zig");
    17	const gather = @import("gather.zig");
    18	const prune = @import("prune.zig");
    19	const refine = @import("refine.zig");
    20	
    21	pub const SearchParams = struct {
    22	    kappa_c: u32,
    23	    kappa_d: u32,
    24	    alpha: ?f32 = null,
    25	    top_k: u32,
    26	};
    27	
    28	pub const ScoredDoc = struct {
    29	    doc_id: u32,
    30	    score: f32,
    31	};
    32	
    33	pub const SearchError = error{
    34	    TopKExceedsKappaD,
    35	} || gather.GatherError || pq_mod.PqError || refine.RefineError;
    36	
    37	fn cmpDescScored(_: void, a: ScoredDoc, b: ScoredDoc) bool {
    38	    return a.score > b.score;
    39	}
    40	
    41	/// Run the full paper §5 retrieval pipeline.
    42	///
    43	/// Returns an allocator-owned slice of `≤ top_k` ScoredDocs sorted descending
    44	/// by exact MaxSim. Caller frees with `gpa.free(result)`.
    45	///
    46	/// Determinism: gather emits the same candidate set for a given query/index;
    47	/// prune sorts deterministically; refine is pure; final sort uses strict `>`
    48	/// so equal-score ties keep input order. Sticky doc-id tie-breaks would
    49	/// need a secondary key — defer until benchmarks reveal a need.
    50	pub fn search(
    51	    index: *const storage.Index,
    52	    query_tokens: []const f32,
    53	    n_q: u32,
    54	    params: SearchParams,
    55	    gpa: Allocator,
    56	) SearchError![]ScoredDoc {
    57	    if (params.top_k > params.kappa_d) return error.TopKExceedsKappaD;
    58	
    59	    // Gather (paper §5.1): walk inverted lists, accumulate S̃(q, d).
    60	    const candidates = try gather.gather(
    61	        index,
    62	        query_tokens,
    63	        n_q,
    64	        .{ .kappa_c = params.kappa_c },
    65	        gpa,
    66	    );
    67	    defer gpa.free(candidates);
    68	
    69	    // Prune (paper §5.2): top-κ_d truncation + optional adaptive CP.
    70	    const survivors = prune.prune(candidates, .{
    71	        .kappa_d = params.kappa_d,
    72	        .alpha = params.alpha,
    73	    });
    74	    if (survivors.len == 0) return gpa.alloc(ScoredDoc, 0);
    75	
    76	    // Build the per-query distance table once; shared across all refine calls.
    77	    const table_len: usize =
    78	        @as(usize, constants.PQ_M) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) * @as(usize, n_q);
    79	    const table = try gpa.alloc(f32, table_len);
    80	    defer gpa.free(table);
    81	    try index.pq.buildDistanceTable(query_tokens, n_q, table);
    82	
    83	    // Refine (paper §5.3): exact MaxSim per surviving candidate.
    84	    var refined = try gpa.alloc(ScoredDoc, survivors.len);
    85	    errdefer gpa.free(refined);
    86	    const per_token_max = try gpa.alloc(f32, n_q);
    87	    defer gpa.free(per_token_max);
    88	
    89	    for (survivors, 0..) |s, k| {
    90	        refined[k] = .{
    91	            .doc_id = s.doc_id,
    92	            .score = try refine.refine(index, table, query_tokens, n_q, s, per_token_max),
    93	        };
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    std.sort.pdq(ScoredDoc, refined, {}, cmpDescScored);
    97	
    98	    // Trim to top_k.
    99	    const want: usize = @min(refined.len, @as(usize, params.top_k));
   100	    if (want < refined.len) {
   101	        const trimmed = try gpa.alloc(ScoredDoc, want);
   102	        @memcpy(trimmed, refined[0..want]);
   103	        gpa.free(refined);
   104	        return trimmed;
   105	    }

    19	## Measured performance (Apple Silicon, 10 cores)
    20	
    21	Two corpora on a real Czech Jira ticket archive, `jinaai/jina-colbert-v2-64`, dim=64.
    22	
    23	### 2,000-doc chunk (117,645 token vectors, kappa=10,240, θ=10 paper-relax)
    24	
    25	| Build phase       | 1 thread | 10 threads | Speedup |
    26	|-------------------|---------:|-----------:|--------:|
    27	| `tac.clusterFlat` |    218 ms |     321 ms | —    |
    28	| residuals + norms |      9 ms |       4 ms | 2.4× |
    29	| **`pq.train`**    |  42,575 ms |  **9,011 ms** | **4.7×** |
    30	| `pq.encode`       |    820 ms |     296 ms | 2.8× |
    31	| **`hnsw.build`**  |  3,838 ms |   **867 ms** | **4.4×** |
    32	| serialise         |     16 ms |      24 ms | —    |
    33	| **Total**         |  **47,477 ms** |  **10,527 ms** | **4.5×** |
    34	
    35	### Full 26,678-doc corpus (1,521,797 token vectors, kappa=32,768, paper-strict μ/τ/ε/θ)
    36	
    37	19,221 distinct token IDs < 39,020 paper-strict max → no relaxation needed.
    38	
    39	| Phase | Time | Notes |
    40	|---|---:|---|
    41	| Metal encode (pylate, fp32, batch=64) | **48 min** | GPU-bound; FP16 + larger batch is 4-6× ahead |
    42	| `tac.clusterFlat` (parallel) | 4.2 s | comptime-specialized distance kernels ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)) — 1.28× over the generic @Vector path |
    43	| residuals + norms (parallel) | 24 ms | |
    44	| **`pq.train` (parallel + work-stealing outer dispatch + comptime-specialized kernels)** | **74.9 s** | atomic-counter outer dispatch ([plan-10](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-10-pq-workstealing.plan.md)) plus comptime-specialized `vec.l2sq` for hot dims ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)) — every PQ encode/refit triple `(subspace × centroid × token)` hits `vec.l2sq` on a 2-element subspace; the inline dispatcher lets the call-site `a.len` propagate through the switch and DCE the rest. ~12 % off vs work-stealing-only baseline; byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10} preserved. |
    45	| `pq.encode` (parallel) | 0.9 s | |
    46	| **`hnsw.build` (parallel, 10 threads)** | **2.7 s** | chunked deferred-commit, paper-strict deterministic CSR ([plan-06](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-06-hnsw-parallel.plan.md)) |
    47	| serialise | 0.15 s | |
    48	| **Build total (10 threads)** | **1 min 23 s** | work-stealing pq.train ([plan-10](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-10-pq-workstealing.plan.md)) + comptime-specialized distance kernels ([plan-11](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-11-vec-simd-specialization.plan.md)). 1.13× over the work-stealing-only baseline (1:34); index byte-equal vs the generic kernel. |
    49	| **Search latency** | **~54 ms / query** | paper-strict single-core, `kappa_c=80, kappa_d=1000` |
    50	| **Index size** | **75.8 MB** | for 26,678 Jira tickets |
    51	
    52	Quality smoke on Czech queries:
    53	- `"rozesílání hromadné pošty z eshop domény"` → top hit `jira:ABC-1` (DKIM/SPF newsletter ticket) — matches WARP / `setup-note.md` ground truth.
    54	- 0 docs dropped during encode — pylate's ColBERTv2 path handles all 26k Jira tickets including Czech multilingual content cleanly.
    55	
    56	### Optimization story
    57	
    58	Build was 47.5 s single-thread on the 2 k chunk; we got it to 12.4 s by parallelising the four embarrassingly-parallel stages (TAC per-token, PQ train per-subspace, PQ encode per-token, residuals per-token). HNSW build was the next lever — at 26 k it was 17.0 s serial; we replaced it with a chunked deferred-commit parallel build (paper-strict deterministic) which lands at **2.8 s on 10 threads (≈6× speedup)**, saving 14 s on full-corpus build wall time. PQ training stayed dominant because M=32 subspaces / 10 threads with a static `chunk = ceil(32/10) = 4` left fast workers idle while slow ones ground out the last subspace in their chunk. **Plan-10** swapped the static chunker for a shared `std.atomic.Value(u32)` work-stealing counter — every worker pulls the next subspace via `fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index reaches `PQ_M`. Lands at **97.1 s on full-Jira (was 115.8 s, ≈16 % off)**; the gain is bounded because most subspaces converge in similar Lloyd-iter counts, so the slack the static chunker left on the table was real but smaller than the 30-40 % ceiling. **Plan-11** then comptime-specialized the distance kernels (`vec.dot`, `vec.l2sq`, `vec.normalizeInPlace`) for the four dims that actually appear in the codebase — 2/4 (PQ subspaces) and 64/128 (jina-colbert-v2-64, ColBERTv2.0). **Full-Jira build dropped from 1:34 → 1:23 (1.13×, ≈12 % off total wall time), with `pq.train` itself going 84.8 s → 74.9 s.** Index file is byte-equal vs the generic kernel (paper §9 single-thread retrieval semantics unchanged) and build is byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10}. Supporting microbench (median of 5, kmeans-assign k=256, the dominant `vec.l2sq` caller in pq.train): dim=2 1.14×, dim=64 1.53×, dim=128 1.42× — the per-call wins are larger than the full-build delta because the kernels also amortize fixed pq.train scaffolding (codebook init, partition counts, Lloyd convergence detection) that didn't get faster. The remaining levers, in order of payoff:
    59	
    60	1. **Within-subspace k-means parallelism** — *infrastructure shipped ([plan-07](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-07-within-subspace-kmeans.plan.md))* but inactive at the typical 10-thread invocation: `kmeans.fit` takes `n_threads` and parallelises the per-vector argmin across static chunks while keeping the f32 reduction order serial-in-vector-order, so n_threads=1 ↔ n_threads=10 produce **byte-equal** centroids (paper-strict). At `--threads 10` on M=32 / 10 cores the subspace-level parallelism already saturates; passing more inner threads only oversubscribes. The within-subspace path gates on `n_threads > M`, so it activates when a user runs e.g. `--threads 64`.
    61	2. **FP16 Metal inference + bigger batches in the encoder** — 4-6× on the 48-minute encode; the single biggest absolute win since encode is 95 % of total ingestion cost.
    62	
    63	#### Why `pub inline fn` matters for the dispatcher
    64	
    65	The plan-11 dispatcher in `src/util/vec.zig` looks innocuous:
    66	
    88	
    89	## Strict-paper rule
    90	
    91	When the paper specifies a parameter, **use that value exactly**. Defaults:
    92	
    93	- TAC: `μ=128, τ=256, ε=4, θ=39`
    94	- PQ: `M=32 subspaces, b=8 bits`
    95	- HNSW: `M_hnsw=32, efc=1500, ef_s=1.5·κ_c`
    96	- Retrieval grid: `κ_c ∈ {15,20,40,80,100,120}`, `κ_d ∈ {250,500,1000,2000,4000}`, `α ∈ {0.35,0.4,0.45,0.5}`
    97	
    98	Where the paper is silent (e.g. tie-breaking in Phase 4 reconciliation, exact CP update rule), each implementation choice is documented in the source comment block beside it as a `# paper-gap:` note.
    99	
   100	## Workflow
   101	
   102	- **Push directly to `main`** once `zig build test` is green. No PR review for this repo.

    25	## Encoder dtype + batch size (post-hackathon plan 08)
    26	
    27	`tools/encode.py` exposes two flags whose auto-defaults follow `--device`:
    28	
    29	| flag | cpu / cuda | mps |
    30	|---|---|---|
    31	| `--dtype` | fp32 | fp16 (`model.half()` after load) |
    32	| `--encoder-batch-size` | 32 | 256 |
    33	
    34	`--dtype auto` (default) picks fp16 only on `--device mps`; cpu/cuda stay
    35	fp32. Pass `--dtype fp32` explicitly to opt out on MPS. tokens.bin is f32
    36	regardless — the per-doc loop already does `.cpu().float()` before
    37	normalisation, so the on-disk format is unchanged. We also run a 4-doc
    38	warmup pass on MPS so the first real batch isn't measuring Metal kernel
    39	compilation.
    40	
    41	Pattern mirrors sibling `services/warp-service/server.py` lines 187–231,
    42	which already runs `WARP_ENCODER_FP16=1` + `WARP_ENCODER_BATCH_SIZE=256`
    43	on the same Apple Silicon hardware.
    44	
    45	### Measured speedup (Apple Silicon, jina-colbert-v2-64)
    46	
    47	End-to-end CLI wall-clock on `tests/fixtures/live/docs.jsonl` (100 docs,
    48	1697 kept tokens), back-to-back so HF cache is warm:
    49	
    50	| invocation                                 | wall   | speedup |
    51	|--------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
    52	| fp32, encoder-batch-size 32                | 12.4 s | 1.00×   |
    53	| fp16, encoder-batch-size 256               |  9.6 s | 1.29×   |
    54

    56

    63	this hardware / model: the live fixture is too small to saturate the GPU
    64	(model load dominates wall-clock), and on the longer Jira docs the
    65	encoder is bandwidth-bound rather than compute-bound. The speedup is
    66	real but in the 1.3-2× band, not 4-6×. Worth re-measuring on the full
    67	corpus + a larger ColBERT model where the matmuls dominate.

   188	## MLX path setup (post-hackathon plan-12)
   189	
   190	`tools/encode_mlx.py` is an additive Apple-Silicon-native re-port of
   191	encode.py via Apple's MLX framework. The 4-gate parity contract in
   192	`tests/live/mlx_parity.py` is **green** at both fp32 and fp16 on the
   193	100-doc fixture, with gates 1+2 also confirmed on 1000 real Jira docs.
   194	Encode.py remains the cross-platform canonical path; encode_mlx.py is
   195	the fast Apple-Silicon option.
   196	
   197	### Dependencies
   198	
   199	Two extra packages on top of the pylate set, gated to darwin/arm64 in
   200	`tools/requirements.txt`:
   253	### Parity gate results (100-doc fixture)
   254	
   255	PyTorch fp32 CPU baseline vs MLX fp32:
   256	
   257	| gate | result |
   258	|---|---|
   259	| 1. token-id byte equality                  | PASS — 1592 ids match exactly |
   260	| 2. cosine ≥ 0.998 mean (≥ 0.99 min)        | PASS — mean 0.999998, min 0.999923, p1 0.999991 |
   261	| 3. two-run MLX determinism                 | PASS — 203776 floats BYTE-IDENTICAL |
   262	| 4. downstream TAC cluster overlap          | PASS — kappa=92 centroid cosine mean 0.999999, min 0.999997 |
   263	
   264	PyTorch fp32 CPU baseline vs MLX **fp16**:
   265	
   266	| gate | result |
   267	|---|---|
   268	| 1. token-id byte equality                  | PASS |
   269	| 2. cosine ≥ 0.998 mean (≥ 0.99 min)        | PASS — mean 0.999999, min 0.999873, p1 0.999997 |
   270	| 3. two-run MLX determinism                 | PASS — 203776 floats BYTE-IDENTICAL even at fp16 |
   271	| 4. downstream TAC cluster overlap          | PASS — centroid cosine mean 0.999999, min 0.999998 |
   272	
   273	Cross-corpus spot-check on 1000 real Jira docs (gates 1 + 2 only):
   274	
   275	| gate | mlx-fp32 | mlx-fp16 |
   276	|---|---|---|
   277	| 1. token-id byte equality      | PASS (55202 ids) | PASS (55202 ids) |
   278	| 2. cosine                      | mean 0.999997, min 0.999803 | mean 0.999998, min 0.999970 |
   279	
   280	Notable: **MLX is byte-deterministic across runs** at both fp32 and fp16
   281	on Apple Silicon. encode.py under the current pin set (transformers 4.57)
   282	is NOT — two back-to-back runs differ in ~99% of output floats with max
   283	abs ~1.5e-6 (fp32 ε-class) because transformers' `mean_resizing` path
   284	fires when pylate expands the BERT vocab to add ColBERT's `[D]` and
   285	`[Q]` tokens, advancing the global RNG without seeding before any
   286	forward pass. That drift is below the parity floor so it doesn't break
   287	gate 2, but it does mean MLX is now the more reproducible backend.
   288	
   289	### Measured speedup (post-plan-13)
   290	
   291	End-to-end CLI wall-clock, back-to-back so HF cache + MLX kernels are warm.
   292	Re-measured after plan-13 perf tuning (fused SDPA, dtype-specific weights,
   293	larger batch defaults + opt-in --sort-by-length, vectorized keep gather +
   294	bulk-write writer).
   295	
   296	100-doc live fixture (1592 kept tokens, mostly short synthetic English):
   297	
   298	| invocation                                                 | wall   | speedup |
   299	|------------------------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
   300	| encode.py fp32 cpu, batch=32                               |  4.10s | 1.00×   |
   301	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 (default, post-plan-13)      |  3.86s | 1.06×   |
   302	
   303	1000-doc Jira subset (55202 kept tokens, avg ~2.7 kB per doc):
   304	
   305	| invocation                                                 | wall   | speedup |
   306	|------------------------------------------------------------|--------|---------|
   307	| encode.py fp32 cpu, batch=32                               | 35.6s  | 1.00×   |
   308	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=32 (parity-harness config)       | 10.4s  | 3.4×    |
   309	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 (default, post-plan-13)      | 10.1s  | 3.5×    |
   310	| encode_mlx.py fp16, batch=128 + --sort-by-length           |  5.7s  | **6.2×** |
   311	
   312	Apple Silicon thermals are noisy across back-to-back runs (a sustained
   313	encode loop heats the SoC and back-to-back wall-clocks drift up by 30-50%
   314	once the system throttles). The numbers above are the median of three
   315	runs after a cooldown gap; numerically equivalent runs on a previously
   316	hot SoC come out ~30-50% slower.
   317	
   318	The 100-doc fixture is too small to amortize tokenizer + cold-start cost

    44	/// Best-effort pin the calling thread to a single core (paper §9 reports
    45	/// retrieval as single-core wall clock).
    46	///
    47	/// - Linux: `sched_setaffinity(0, [1u << core])` on a singleton CPU set.
    48	/// - macOS: thread_policy_set with THREAD_AFFINITY_POLICY (Apple Silicon
    49	///   ignores real affinity; the tag still groups co-scheduled threads).
    50	/// - Other OS: no-op.
    51	///
    52	/// Errors are swallowed — pinning is best-effort; benchmark numbers are
    53	/// still recorded if the syscall fails. Useful out-of-band signal: the
    54	/// per-stage latency variance in the Report will be wider on a busy
    55	/// machine when affinity didn't take.
    56	pub fn pinToCore(core: u32) void {
    57	    pinToCoreImpl(core);
    58	}
    59	
    60	const pinToCoreImpl = if (builtin.os.tag == .linux) pinToCoreLinux else pinToCoreNoop;
    61	
    62	fn pinToCoreLinux(core: u32) void {
    63	    // CPU set type matches the kernel's cpu_set_t (1024 bits).
    64	    var set: std.posix.cpu_set_t = std.mem.zeroes(std.posix.cpu_set_t);
   120	
   121	    var sum_total: u64 = 0;
   122	    var sum_gather: u64 = 0;
   123	    var sum_prune: u64 = 0;
   124	    var sum_table: u64 = 0;
   125	    var sum_refine: u64 = 0;
   126	    for (timings) |t| {
   127	        sum_total += t.total_ns;
   128	        sum_gather += t.gather_ns;
   129	        sum_prune += t.prune_ns;
   130	        sum_table += t.table_build_ns;
   131	        sum_refine += t.refine_ns;
   132	    }
   133	    const n: f64 = @floatFromInt(timings.len);
   134	
   135	    var stack_buf: [16384]u64 = undefined;
   136	    const slice = if (timings.len <= stack_buf.len)
   137	        stack_buf[0..timings.len]
   138	    else blk: {
   139	        const heap = std.heap.page_allocator.alloc(u64, timings.len) catch
   140	            @panic("latency.report: allocation failed");
   141	        break :blk heap;
   142	    };
   143	    defer if (timings.len > stack_buf.len) std.heap.page_allocator.free(slice);
   144	
   145	    for (timings, 0..) |t, i| slice[i] = t.total_ns;
   146	    std.mem.sort(u64, slice, {}, std.sort.asc(u64));
   147	
   148	    const p50_idx = (timings.len * 50) / 100;
   149	    const p95_idx_raw = (timings.len * 95) / 100;
   150	    const p95_idx = if (p95_idx_raw >= timings.len) timings.len - 1 else p95_idx_raw;

README.md:58:Build was 47.5 s single-thread on the 2 k chunk; we got it to 12.4 s by parallelising the four embarrassingly-parallel stages (TAC per-token, PQ train per-subspace, PQ encode per-token, residuals per-token). HNSW build was the next lever — at 26 k it was 17.0 s serial; we replaced it with a chunked deferred-commit parallel build (paper-strict deterministic) which lands at **2.8 s on 10 threads (≈6× speedup)**, saving 14 s on full-corpus build wall time. PQ training stayed dominant because M=32 subspaces / 10 threads with a static `chunk = ceil(32/10) = 4` left fast workers idle while slow ones ground out the last subspace in their chunk. **Plan-10** swapped the static chunker for a shared `std.atomic.Value(u32)` work-stealing counter — every worker pulls the next subspace via `fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index reaches `PQ_M`. Lands at **97.1 s on full-Jira (was 115.8 s, ≈16 % off)**; the gain is bounded because most subspaces converge in similar Lloyd-iter counts, so the slack the static chunker left on the table was real but smaller than the 30-40 % ceiling. **Plan-11** then comptime-specialized the distance kernels (`vec.dot`, `vec.l2sq`, `vec.normalizeInPlace`) for the four dims that actually appear in the codebase — 2/4 (PQ subspaces) and 64/128 (jina-colbert-v2-64, ColBERTv2.0). **Full-Jira build dropped from 1:34 → 1:23 (1.13×, ≈12 % off total wall time), with `pq.train` itself going 84.8 s → 74.9 s.** Index file is byte-equal vs the generic kernel (paper §9 single-thread retrieval semantics unchanged) and build is byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10}. Supporting microbench (median of 5, kmeans-assign k=256, the dominant `vec.l2sq` caller in pq.train): dim=2 1.14×, dim=64 1.53×, dim=128 1.42× — the per-call wins are larger than the full-build delta because the kernels also amortize fixed pq.train scaffolding (codebook init, partition counts, Lloyd convergence detection) that didn't get faster. The remaining levers, in order of payoff:
README.md:60:1. **Within-subspace k-means parallelism** — *infrastructure shipped ([plan-07](.codex/plans/post-hackathon-07-within-subspace-kmeans.plan.md))* but inactive at the typical 10-thread invocation: `kmeans.fit` takes `n_threads` and parallelises the per-vector argmin across static chunks while keeping the f32 reduction order serial-in-vector-order, so n_threads=1 ↔ n_threads=10 produce **byte-equal** centroids (paper-strict). At `--threads 10` on M=32 / 10 cores the subspace-level parallelism already saturates; passing more inner threads only oversubscribes. The within-subspace path gates on `n_threads > M`, so it activates when a user runs e.g. `--threads 64`.

    58	    /// Encode a single residual into M = 32 codes (one byte per subspace).
    59	    pub fn encode(self: *const PQ, residual: []const f32, out: *[constants.PQ_M]u8) PqError!void {
    60	        if (residual.len != @as(usize, self.dim)) return error.DimMismatch;
    61	        var m: u32 = 0;
    62	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
    63	            const sub = residual[m * self.sub_dim ..][0..self.sub_dim];
    64	            var best_c: u32 = 0;
    65	            var best_d: f32 = std.math.inf(f32);
    66	            var c: u32 = 0;
    67	            while (c < constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) : (c += 1) {
    68	                const d = vec.l2sq(sub, self.codebookEntry(m, c)) catch unreachable;
    69	                if (d < best_d) {
    70	                    best_d = d;
    71	                    best_c = c;
    72	                }
    73	            }
    74	            out[m] = @intCast(best_c);
    75	        }
   108	        while (m < constants.PQ_M) : (m += 1) {
   109	            var c: u32 = 0;
   110	            while (c < constants.PQ_CENTROIDS) : (c += 1) {
   111	                const code_vec = self.codebookEntry(m, c);
   112	                const base = ((@as(usize, m) * @as(usize, constants.PQ_CENTROIDS)) +
   113	                    @as(usize, c)) * @as(usize, n_q);
   114	                var i: u32 = 0;
   115	                while (i < n_q) : (i += 1) {
   116	                    const q_sub = query_tokens[
   117	                        @as(usize, i) * @as(usize, self.dim) + m * self.sub_dim ..
   118	                    ][0..self.sub_dim];
   119	                    out[base + @as(usize, i)] = vec.dot(q_sub, code_vec) catch unreachable;
   120	                }
   121	            }
