   118	    /// Half-open `[lo, hi)` global token range of doc `d`. Mirrors the
   740	
   741	/// Parse a serialised index back into structured form. Allocates copies of
   742	/// the PQ codebooks, HNSW CSR arrays, and inverted-list buffers because the
   743	/// downstream types own their memory — that keeps mmap unmaps decoupled
   744	/// from the lifetime of those structs. Doc-index, doc-payload, centroids,
   745	/// and residual-norms slices alias `bytes` (no copy) since they're plain
   746	/// arrays read at query time.
   747	///
   748	/// `bytes` must be 8-byte aligned at offset 0 — both `gpa.alloc(u8, ...)`
   749	/// and any mmap region trivially are. With `header_region_size = 128` and
   750	/// every section padded to 8B, the natural alignments line up.
   751	pub fn parse(bytes: []align(8) const u8, gpa: Allocator) StorageError!Index {
   752	    if (bytes.len < header_region_size + 8) return error.InvalidIndex;
   753	
   754	    // Read header.
   755	    var hdr: Header = undefined;
   756	    @memcpy(std.mem.asBytes(&hdr), bytes[0..@sizeOf(Header)]);
   757	
   758	    if (!std.mem.eql(u8, &hdr.magic, &constants.INDEX_MAGIC)) {
   759	        return error.InvalidIndex;
   760	    }
   761	    if (hdr.version != constants.INDEX_VERSION) return error.UnsupportedVersion;
   762	    if (hdr.pq_M != constants.PQ_M) return error.InvalidIndex;
   763	    if (hdr.pq_bits != @as(u8, @intCast(constants.PQ_BITS))) return error.InvalidIndex;
   764	    if (hdr.sub_dim * hdr.pq_M != hdr.dim) return error.InvalidIndex;
   765	    if (hdr.dim == 0 or hdr.kappa == 0) return error.InvalidIndex;
   766	
   767	    // Section offsets must be monotonic and within bounds.
   768	    const offs = [_]u64{
   769	        hdr.centroids_off, hdr.hnsw_off,    hdr.pq_off,
   770	        hdr.ilist_off,     hdr.doc_off,     hdr.doc_tok_off,
   771	        hdr.norms_off,     hdr.footer_off,
   772	    };
   773	    if (offs[0] != header_region_size) return error.InvalidIndex;
   774	    var i: usize = 1;
   775	    while (i < offs.len) : (i += 1) {
   776	        if (offs[i] < offs[i - 1]) return error.InvalidIndex;
   777	        if (offs[i] > bytes.len) return error.InvalidIndex;
   778	    }
   779	    if (hdr.footer_off + 8 > bytes.len) return error.InvalidIndex;
   780	
   781	    // CRC check.
   782	    const stored_crc: u32 = readU32Le(bytes, @intCast(hdr.footer_off));
   783	    var hasher = std.hash.Crc32.init();
   784	    hasher.update(bytes[0..@intCast(hdr.footer_off)]);
   785	    if (hasher.final() != stored_crc) return error.IndexCorrupt;
   786	
   787	    // ---- Centroids (alias) ----
   788	    const centroids_bytes = bytes[@intCast(hdr.centroids_off)..@intCast(hdr.hnsw_off)];
   789	    const centroids_n: usize = @intCast(@as(u64, hdr.kappa) * @as(u64, hdr.dim));
   790	    if (centroids_bytes.len < centroids_n * @sizeOf(f32)) return error.InvalidIndex;

README.md:58:Build was 47.5 s single-thread on the 2 k chunk; we got it to 12.4 s by parallelising the four embarrassingly-parallel stages (TAC per-token, PQ train per-subspace, PQ encode per-token, residuals per-token). HNSW build was the next lever — at 26 k it was 17.0 s serial; we replaced it with a chunked deferred-commit parallel build (paper-strict deterministic) which lands at **2.8 s on 10 threads (≈6× speedup)**, saving 14 s on full-corpus build wall time. PQ training stayed dominant because M=32 subspaces / 10 threads with a static `chunk = ceil(32/10) = 4` left fast workers idle while slow ones ground out the last subspace in their chunk. **Plan-10** swapped the static chunker for a shared `std.atomic.Value(u32)` work-stealing counter — every worker pulls the next subspace via `fetchAdd(1, .monotonic)` until the index reaches `PQ_M`. Lands at **97.1 s on full-Jira (was 115.8 s, ≈16 % off)**; the gain is bounded because most subspaces converge in similar Lloyd-iter counts, so the slack the static chunker left on the table was real but smaller than the 30-40 % ceiling. **Plan-11** then comptime-specialized the distance kernels (`vec.dot`, `vec.l2sq`, `vec.normalizeInPlace`) for the four dims that actually appear in the codebase — 2/4 (PQ subspaces) and 64/128 (jina-colbert-v2-64, ColBERTv2.0). **Full-Jira build dropped from 1:34 → 1:23 (1.13×, ≈12 % off total wall time), with `pq.train` itself going 84.8 s → 74.9 s.** Index file is byte-equal vs the generic kernel (paper §9 single-thread retrieval semantics unchanged) and build is byte-equal across n_threads ∈ {1, 4, 10}. Supporting microbench (median of 5, kmeans-assign k=256, the dominant `vec.l2sq` caller in pq.train): dim=2 1.14×, dim=64 1.53×, dim=128 1.42× — the per-call wins are larger than the full-build delta because the kernels also amortize fixed pq.train scaffolding (codebook init, partition counts, Lloyd convergence detection) that didn't get faster. The remaining levers, in order of payoff:
README.md:78:The `inline` is load-bearing. Without it, `switch (a.len)` is a runtime branch — and dim=2 (the PQ-subspace path that pq.train hits **billions of times**) regressed from **112 ns → 207 ns per kmeans-assign step (1.85× slower)** even with the dim=2 arm picking the comptime kernel. Adding `pub inline fn` lets the call-site's slice length (which pq.train's inner loop knows is constant 2) propagate through the switch; LLVM dead-code-eliminates every other arm and emits two scalar mul-adds. With `inline` the same workload is **98 ns (1.14× faster than the original generic kernel).** Same source, same kernels — only difference is whether the dispatcher can be folded into the caller. This is the kind of win that doesn't show up on a single-call microbench (where `a` and `b` are loop-invariant pointers and LLVM can hoist anything) but dominates real hot loops where the slice length is a compile-time constant at the call site but not at the function definition.

/bin/zsh -lc "nl -ba docs/paper.md | sed -n '90,116p'" in /Users/satan/side/experiments/ir-multivector-retrieval
 succeeded in 0ms:
    90	
    91	### 5.1 Gather (centroid-only)
    92	
    93	For each query token `q_i`:
    94	- HNSW search → top-`κ_c` centroids; `ef_s = 1.5 · κ_c`.
    95	- Walk inverted lists of those centroids, accumulating per-document max:
    96	  ```
    97	  s̃_i(d) = max_{ j : d ∈ L_j }  ⟨q_i, c_j⟩
    98	  ```
    99	- Sum across query tokens:
   100	  ```
   101	  S̃(q, d) = Σ_{i=1..n_q}  s̃_i(d)
   102	  ```
   103	
   104	This avoids touching any per-token PQ code in the gather phase.
   105	
   106	### 5.2 Pruning
   107	
   108	Rank candidates by `S̃`; truncate to top-`κ_d`. Optional adaptive Candidates Pruning (CP) using a fraction `α` of the running maximum (`α ∈ {0.35, 0.4, 0.45, 0.5}`).
   109	
   110	### 5.3 Refine
   111	
   112	For surviving candidates, compute exact MaxSim using centroids + PQ-decompressed residuals.
   113	
   114	**Cache-optimized distance table.** Three-level layout to give every per-token lookup contiguous bytes:
   115	- Macro-blocks indexed by PQ subspace (M = 32 macro-blocks).

  1057	    var out: std.ArrayList(u32) = .empty;
  1058	    errdefer out.deinit(gpa);
  1059	    try out.ensureTotalCapacity(gpa, m);
  1060	
  1061	    for (sorted) |cand| {
  1062	        if (out.items.len >= m) break;
  1063	        // Keep cand iff it's closer to q than to every already-chosen r.
  1064	        var keep = true;
  1065	        const cand_vec = sliceVec(centroids, dim, cand.node);
  1066	        for (out.items) |r_id| {
  1067	            const r_vec = sliceVec(centroids, dim, r_id);

  1069	            if (sim_cr > cand.sim) {
  1070	                keep = false;
  1071	                break;
  1072	            }
  1073	        }
  1074	        if (keep) try out.append(gpa, cand.node);
  1075	    }
  1076

  1078	}

codex
Used `$codebase-deep-research`. I ran `zig build test --summary all`; it reports `201/201 tests passed`.

**Findings**
1. **Critical | correctness/architecture** | Evidence: `src/index/pq.zig:214`, `src/index/pq.zig:222`, `src/index/pq.zig:263`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:273`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:317`, `src/tac/tac.zig:737`, `src/tac/tac.zig:763`, `src/tac/tac.zig:800`, `src/index/storage.zig:226`.  
   Parallel workers allocate through the caller allocator and several spawn loops `try std.Thread.spawn` without joining already-started workers on later spawn failure. That can race non-thread-safe allocators and can free worker context while live threads still use it. HNSW has the right cleanup pattern at `src/index/hnsw.zig:865`. Fix by preallocating per-worker scratch or requiring a thread-safe allocator, and add `spawned`/`errdefer join` everywhere. Effort: **M**.

2. **Critical | correctness** | Evidence: `src/tac/kmeans.zig:188`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:194`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:197`.  
   Lloyd convergence is checked after assignment/accumulation but before centroid update. If assignments change with small WCSS delta, the returned `assignments` and `wcss` describe the new assignment, while `centroids` are still from the previous iteration. Fix by moving convergence after the update or doing a final update before break, then add a test that returned centroids equal assignment means. Effort: **S-M**.

3. **Serious | correctness/honest-reporting** | Evidence: `README.md:58`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:83`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:88`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:296`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:300`, `src/index/storage.zig:399`, `src/index/storage.zig:402`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:1381`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:1387`.  
   The README claims full build byte-equality across `n_threads ∈ {1,4,10}`, but HNSW explicitly documents and tests only `n_threads >= 2` byte-equality; serial is a different deterministic graph. Storage forwards global `--threads` into HNSW, so the full `.tac` image cannot be byte-equal for `1` vs `4/10`. Fix either the claim/tests or the algorithm. Effort: **S** for docs/tests, **L** for serial-equivalent parallel HNSW.

4. **Serious | correctness** | Evidence: `tools/export_colbert_to_mlx.py:196`, `tools/export_colbert_to_mlx.py:291`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:138`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:169`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:196`.  
   The exporter records HuggingFace `layer_norm_eps`, but the MLX BERT blocks instantiate `nn.LayerNorm(hidden)` without passing it. BERT configs commonly use tiny eps values, so this is a real semantic drift hidden by cosine parity. Fix by threading `cfg["layer_norm_eps"]` into all LayerNorms and rerunning parity. Effort: **S**.

5. **Serious | correctness/data-integrity** | Evidence: `src/main.zig:148`, `src/main.zig:750`, `src/main.zig:756`, `tools/_tokens_bin.py:88`, `tools/_tokens_bin.py:89`, `tools/_tokens_bin.py:209`.  
   `tac index`, `tokens.bin`, and metadata writers write directly to the final path. A mid-write error leaves a truncated/corrupt final artifact. Fix temp-file-in-same-dir, flush/fsync, atomic rename, `errdefer` cleanup. Effort: **S**.

6. **Serious | correctness/hygiene** | Evidence: `tools/encode.py:284`, `tools/encode.py:330`, `tools/encode.py:333`, `tools/encode.py:386`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:465`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:468`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:499`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:501`.  
   Encoder batch failures are converted into dropped docs and the process exits successfully as long as one doc encoded. In a service-style invocation this can silently shrink the corpus unless callers parse stderr/metadata. Fix with `--allow-drops`, default fail-fast, and a max drop rate. Effort: **S**.

7. **Serious | correctness/honest-reporting** | Evidence: `tools/encode.py:168`, `tools/encode.py:174`, `tools/encode.py:391`, `tools/encode.py:407`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:91`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:97`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:437`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:499`.  
   PyTorch `--sort-by-length` claims it restores input order, but the implementation explicitly leaves output in encode order. MLX does restore slots by original index. Fix PyTorch to slot-fill like MLX or change the help/docs. Effort: **S**.

8. **Serious | perf/architecture** | Evidence: `src/retrieval/gather.zig:71`, `src/retrieval/gather.zig:77`, `src/retrieval/gather.zig:80`.  
   Gather allocates dense `n_docs` buffers per query and clears two of them globally. Fine for 26k Jira docs, but this is not the paper-scale hot path; at millions of docs the query path becomes allocation and memory-bandwidth dominated. Fix with reusable query workspace plus generation-stamped sparse arrays. Effort: **M**.

9. **Serious | perf** | Evidence: `docs/paper.md:117`, `docs/paper.md:119`, `src/retrieval/refine.zig:80`, `src/retrieval/refine.zig:82`, `src/retrieval/refine.zig:114`, `src/retrieval/refine.zig:125`, `src/retrieval/refine.zig:130`.  
   Refine has the paper’s distance-table layout, but scoring is still the simpler fused `(doc_token, query_token)` loop and recomputes centroid dot products inside the inner loop. This is likely a real search hotspot behind the `~54 ms/query` number. Fix the paper §5.3 two-pass/cache-optimized scoring and measure stage timings. Effort: **M**.

10. **Serious | perf/honest-reporting** | Evidence: `src/main.zig:18`, `benchmarks/msmarco_v1.zig:92`, `benchmarks/lotte_pooled.zig:82`, `benchmarks/microbench_vec.zig:295`, `README.md:19`, `README.md:49`, `src/eval/latency.zig:48`, `src/eval/latency.zig:60`, `src/retrieval/bench.zig:222`.  
   Perf binaries use `DebugAllocator`, Apple “pin core” is actually a no-op, and the sweep runs fixed-order single passes without warm-up/cooldown. README headline numbers are not reproducible from an exact command, commit, allocator, machine state, and warm-up protocol. Fix perf harness discipline before treating numbers as headline claims. Effort: **M**.

11. **Serious | correctness/test-gap** | Evidence: `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:250`, `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:264`, `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:304`, `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:318`, `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:338`, `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:399`, `tests/live/mlx_parity.py:400`.  
   The MLX parity gates are a useful floor, not an end-to-end guarantee. Gate 2 is cosine-only; gate 4 compares headers and centroid cosine only, not assignments, PQ codebooks, HNSW graph, inverted lists, or search rankings. Add absolute-error/norm gates and a search-ranking parity gate on a fixed query fixture. Effort: **M**.

12. **Serious | correctness/strict-paper** | Evidence: `docs/paper.md:94`, `README.md:95`, `src/constants.zig:24`, `src/constants.zig:26`, `src/constants.zig:35`, `src/constants.zig:37`, `src/retrieval/gather.zig:63`.  
   `ef_s` is internally inconsistent: docs say `1.5·κ_c`, the test name says `ceil`, but implementation floors, and the test expects floor for odd `κ_c=15`. Decide floor vs ceil, document the paper-gap if needed, and update the test name/values. Effort: **S**.

13. **Minor | correctness/perf** | Evidence: `src/retrieval/prune.zig:19`, `src/retrieval/prune.zig:50`, `src/retrieval/prune.zig:55`, `src/retrieval/prune.zig:66`, `src/retrieval/search.zig:46`, `src/retrieval/search.zig:49`, `src/retrieval/search.zig:96`.  
   Sort comparators have no doc-id tie-break, despite comments implying tie stability. Adaptive CP can also drop every candidate if the best score is negative because `alpha * top` is greater than `top`. Add deterministic secondary keys and guard CP for nonpositive top scores. Effort: **S**.

14. **Minor | tech-debt/honest-reporting** | Evidence: `README.md:58`, `src/util/vec.zig:37`, `src/util/vec.zig:109`, `src/util/vec.zig:183`, `src/util/vec.zig:188`.  
   README says `normalizeInPlace` is specialized for dims `2/4/64/128`; code specializes only `64/128`. `dot` and `l2sq` are fine for `2/4/64/128`. Fix docs or add the missing specializations. Effort: **S**.

15. **Minor | architecture/tech-debt** | Evidence: `src/tac/tac.zig:250`, `src/tac/tac.zig:258`, `src/tac/tac.zig:292`, `src/tac/tac.zig:309`, `README.md:27`.  
   Phase 4 calls its surplus rule “canonical Hamilton largest-remainder,” but the implementation repeatedly awards surplus to the same max-fraction token until cap. That may be acceptable as a paper-gap choice, but the name/comment is misleading. Also, TAC parallelism is already slower on the 2k README case. Fix the comment and consider work-stealing over active tokens. Effort: **S-M**.

16. **Minor | honest-reporting** | Evidence: `src/index/hnsw.zig:918`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:927`.  
   Parallel HNSW verbose phase timings leave greedy/search/heuristic accumulators at zero, so per-phase output is misleading in the mode used for headline builds. Report wall-only or aggregate worker timings. Effort: **S**.

**What Looks Fine**
- PQ work-stealing determinism is real in the algorithm: subspace ID drives seed and output location, not completion order (`src/index/pq.zig:187`, `src/index/pq.zig:199`), and tests cover `1/4/10` (`src/index/pq.zig:445`, `src/index/pq.zig:470`).  
- KMeans parallel reduction order is byte-stable by design (`src/tac/kmeans.zig:160`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:183`) and tested (`src/tac/kmeans.zig:430`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:465`), aside from the convergence bug above.  
- HNSW parallel commit order is deterministic, not scheduling-luck: workers fill plans, then the main thread commits in q-order (`src/index/hnsw.zig:841`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:880`). The boundary is `>=2` threads, not `1` vs `>=2`.  
- MLX BERT’s high-level post-LN ordering, mask shape, and position offset are broadly aligned with ordinary HF BERT (`tools/encode_mlx.py:158`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:200`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:237`); the LayerNorm epsilon is the real semantic miss.

**Top 5 To Fix First**
1. Fix parallel worker cleanup and allocator ownership across PQ/KMeans/TAC/storage.
2. Fix KMeans convergence ordering and add centroid-consistency tests.
3. Correct the full-build determinism contract and add a real full-index `n_threads=1/4/10` byte test.
4. Harden encoder correctness: MLX LayerNorm eps, fail-fast drops, PyTorch sort-order restoration, stronger parity gates.
5. Make writes atomic and replace DebugAllocator/no-warmup benchmark paths before publishing more perf claims.
tokens used
357 106
Used `$codebase-deep-research`. I ran `zig build test --summary all`; it reports `201/201 tests passed`.

**Findings**
1. **Critical | correctness/architecture** | Evidence: `src/index/pq.zig:214`, `src/index/pq.zig:222`, `src/index/pq.zig:263`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:273`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:317`, `src/tac/tac.zig:737`, `src/tac/tac.zig:763`, `src/tac/tac.zig:800`, `src/index/storage.zig:226`.  
   Parallel workers allocate through the caller allocator and several spawn loops `try std.Thread.spawn` without joining already-started workers on later spawn failure. That can race non-thread-safe allocators and can free worker context while live threads still use it. HNSW has the right cleanup pattern at `src/index/hnsw.zig:865`. Fix by preallocating per-worker scratch or requiring a thread-safe allocator, and add `spawned`/`errdefer join` everywhere. Effort: **M**.

2. **Critical | correctness** | Evidence: `src/tac/kmeans.zig:188`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:194`, `src/tac/kmeans.zig:197`.  
   Lloyd convergence is checked after assignment/accumulation but before centroid update. If assignments change with small WCSS delta, the returned `assignments` and `wcss` describe the new assignment, while `centroids` are still from the previous iteration. Fix by moving convergence after the update or doing a final update before break, then add a test that returned centroids equal assignment means. Effort: **S-M**.

3. **Serious | correctness/honest-reporting** | Evidence: `README.md:58`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:83`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:88`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:296`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:300`, `src/index/storage.zig:399`, `src/index/storage.zig:402`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:1381`, `src/index/hnsw.zig:1387`.  
   The README claims full build byte-equality across `n_threads ∈ {1,4,10}`, but HNSW explicitly documents and tests only `n_threads >= 2` byte-equality; serial is a different deterministic graph. Storage forwards global `--threads` into HNSW, so the full `.tac` image cannot be byte-equal for `1` vs `4/10`. Fix either the claim/tests or the algorithm. Effort: **S** for docs/tests, **L** for serial-equivalent parallel HNSW.

4. **Serious | correctness** | Evidence: `tools/export_colbert_to_mlx.py:196`, `tools/export_colbert_to_mlx.py:291`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:138`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:169`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:196`.  
   The exporter records HuggingFace `layer_norm_eps`, but the MLX BERT blocks instantiate `nn.LayerNorm(hidden)` without passing it. BERT configs commonly use tiny eps values, so this is a real semantic drift hidden by cosine parity. Fix by threading `cfg["layer_norm_eps"]` into all LayerNorms and rerunning parity. Effort: **S**.

5. **Serious | correctness/data-integrity** | Evidence: `src/main.zig:148`, `src/main.zig:750`, `src/main.zig:756`, `tools/_tokens_bin.py:88`, `tools/_tokens_bin.py:89`, `tools/_tokens_bin.py:209`.  
   `tac index`, `tokens.bin`, and metadata writers write directly to the final path. A mid-write error leaves a truncated/corrupt final artifact. Fix temp-file-in-same-dir, flush/fsync, atomic rename, `errdefer` cleanup. Effort: **S**.

6. **Serious | correctness/hygiene** | Evidence: `tools/encode.py:284`, `tools/encode.py:330`, `tools/encode.py:333`, `tools/encode.py:386`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:465`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:468`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:499`, `tools/encode_mlx.py:501`.  
   Encoder batch failures are converted into dropped docs and the process exits successfully as long as one doc encoded. In a service-style invocation this can silently shrink the corpus unless callers parse stderr/metadata. Fix with `--allow-drops`, default fail-fast, and a max drop rate. Effort: **S**.
